4. **Bound.** At most ≈2 repair passes (cost control, [ADR-023](#adr-023)). On exhaustion, fall through to the honest message ([ADR-055](#adr-055)'s "understood but didn't build", or the verifier's amber, or an over-constraint flag) — never a silent or givens-violating figure.

**Guardrail — reorder/insert, NEVER drop or weaken a given.** "Make it pass the validations" means *reorder and make implicit objects explicit* only. Silently dropping or softening a stated given to force a green figure is the **same cardinal sin as a fixed default** ([ADR-052](#adr-052)): asserting/abandoning something the student never said, off which they would reason falsely. Genuinely contradictory, or under-/over-specified, givens are **not** repairable by ordering — they must surface honestly (over-constraint → flag + keep prior, the [ADR-053](#adr-053)/[ADR-055](#adr-055) path), not as a quietly-reshuffled figure that abandoned a constraint. The repair prompt states this explicitly: *"if the givens cannot all hold, name which conflict — do not omit any."*

**The failure-feedback contract (what the proxy hands back on a retry).** A boolean isn't actionable; the LLM needs to know *which* line/relation failed and *how*. Each validation pass returns a structured record the repair prompt embeds verbatim:

```ts
interface ValidationFeedback {
  attempt: number;                 // 1-based pass index
  commands: string[];              // the ordered candidate that was tried (echoed back)
  outcome: 'ok' | 'not-handled' | 'error' | 'violations';
  notHandled?: string[];           // utterance lines no rule matched → rephrase into vocabulary
  error?: {                        // a step errored / kept-prior (replay lastError)
    atIndex: number;               // which command in `commands`
    message: string;               // e.g. "unresolved dependencies for: A, E"  → strong REORDER signal
  };                               //      "over-constrained: …"                 → genuine-CONFLICT signal
  violations?: GivenViolation[];   // checkGivens output: {relation, ids, message}
}                                  // e.g. "E should lie on circle P (r 3.60) but is 7.42 from its centre"
```

The three outcomes map to three repair moves: `not-handled` → **rephrase** the named line into the supported catalog (the LLM's vocabulary *is* the catalog); `error` with unresolved-deps → **reorder** (a dependency was used before it was defined — the Q4 late-circle case); `error` with over-constrained, or persistent `violations` → either **insert the implicit object / different recipe** that makes the relation hold, or, if nothing can, **report the conflict**. `violations` is the most informative signal because it names the *stated relation* the chosen construction failed to honour (e.g. "the recipe put E off the circle"), pointing the LLM at the missing constraint rather than at syntax.

**Two input modes (consequence).** This argues for a **whole-problem "paste" path** distinct from the incremental one-fact-at-a-time path. Incremental entry stays the **default** for forward constructions (live build, see each step form). The batch path is where reorder + implicit-object inference happen *before* the parser, returning an ordered list — and where the question-text stripping and "numeric data = optional constraint" classification live. (Incremental entry can still trigger a *local* reorder when a single new fact references objects not yet built, but the batch path is where the big reorders pay off.)

**Distinct from [ADR-049](#adr-049).** ADR-049 is the LLM rescuing the **numerical solver** on a figure that *is* constructible but lands in a wrong basin / misses a branch (re-decompose or seed; triggered by an `evaluate` solve-failure). ADR-073 is the LLM rescuing **input order and decomposition** so a figure is *constructible at all* in dependency order (triggered by reading-order/implicit-object mismatch, upstream of solving). Both share the "LLM proposes structure, the engine verifies before display" contract and the ADR-023 cost controls, and they **compose**: a reordered batch may still hit an ADR-049 solve-failure on a pass, handled by that mechanism.

**Testing (no live API — operator policy).** Per CLAUDE.md, the assistant acts as the **Opus oracle**: reason out the ordered command list the LLM *should* emit for Q4 (circle → C,D on it + chord → K on it → tangent-at-K + KB∥CD pin → B free on the tangent → A = BD∩circle → E = BC∩AK), then verify it parses, builds, and passes `checkGivens` through the real `parse → replay → verify` path; the repair loop is exercised by feeding a deliberately mis-ordered candidate and asserting the feedback record names the unresolved dependency. The Q4 sequence becomes a scenario (the operator's actual transcription, LLM step mocked to the canonical commands). Live Haiku only with explicit operator approval.

**Open questions / sequencing.** The live path depends on the **production proxy deploy** (a parked thread) — but the contract, the Opus-oracle tests, and the batch decomposition can be built and validated offline first. The "paste a whole problem" UI is new surface. Whether to *show* the student that a reorder happened (probably invisible — the figure just forms) is open. Two near-term enablers would let even the messy paste land for Q4: an **implicit/circumscribed circle** ("the circle through C, D, K") and a **tangent-at-P ∥ chord ⇒ P = arc-midpoint** recognizer (so the coupled given resolves to a determined point, not a fragile two-constraint solve). Proposed, not built.

**Operator-raised nuances (2026-06-21, folded in from the ideas backlog).** (a) **Make the decomposition visible / verifiable.** Today the operator cannot tell from the GUI whether a complex single sentence is actually being broken into multiple engine steps; even if the reorder stays invisible to a student, a **dev/debug surfacing of the produced ordered command list** (and a confirmation that decomposition ran) is wanted — both to build trust and to verify this path works at all. (b) **Image input is a second front-end to the same decompose path.** A multimodal variant accepts an **image of the problem** (figure + text) and produces the same ordered command list that the engine accepts + the verifier confirms — i.e. ADR-073's decomposition with vision OCR/parsing upstream, not a separate mechanism. Both are still Proposed.

## ADR-074 — A default-extension point is a RECRUITABLE (not eager) DOF; the failure-path recruit FREES THE BLOCKER (closes the R7 step-3 remainder)

**Status:** Accepted (2026-06-21) · built · 1105 tests green, build clean. Closes the deferred remainder of the [ADR-045](#adr-045) R7 step-3 amendment ("the coupled free-vertex+radius convergence — the bagrut `∠ADB=<number>` case — tangled with [ADR-064](#adr-064)"). Reconciles [ADR-052](#adr-052) (no fixed assumptions) with [ADR-064](#adr-064) (a stated-position point is a given). Same spirit as [ADR-073](#adr-073) (the student doesn't manage the engine — here the *engine* flexes the right DOF, not the student).

**The failure (operator-chosen R7 work).** The real bagrut tangent-secant figure with a NUMERIC angle — circle (free R), external tangent apex `A`, secant `AO` cutting at `C,D`, `G` on the extension of `DB`, `AG⊥AD`, `∠ADB=30` — reported `over-constrained: ∠ADB = 30° cannot hold` and sat rigidly at the seed (19.86°) for **every** target angle. The SYMBOLIC `∠ADB=α` form ([ADR-069](#adr-069)) always built; only a stated number jammed.

**Diagnosis — it's BINDING, not convergence (the prior framing was wrong).** Two experiments through the real parse→replay path settled it: (a) drop `AG⊥AD` → `∠ADB=30` reaches **30.00° exactly**; (b) introduce `G` via `AG⊥AD`+collinear instead of the extension command → **30.00° exactly**. So the coupled apex+radius solve *converges fine*; the 2026-06-21 "doesn't converge" conclusion (which led to two reverted solver-seeding patches) was aimed at the wrong layer. A binding-state dump confirmed the real cause: the greedy apply-time `driveOrCheck` ([apply.ts](../src/engine/apply.ts)) gives `AG⊥AD` the free apex `A` (tier-3 free vertex), and `G על המשך DB` freezes `G` at the default extension `t=1.3` — so when `∠ADB` arrives, `A` is claimed and no DOF is left → over-constraint. (In the [ADR-064](#adr-064) figure the *same* `pointOnExtension` construct freezes `D`, and that is exactly right — `∠BOC=2α` must drive the shape, not drag the student's `D`.) **The two figures differ only in surrounding constraints, not structure**, so a per-point flag cannot distinguish "drive G" from "don't drive D" — the audit's "decide binding once jointly" is unavoidable.

**Decision.**
- **A `pointOnExtension` point carries `extension: true`** (a new optional flag on the `point-on-segment` command and the `OnSegmentPoint`). It is **NOT** `free` — so the eager `driveOrCheck` never picks it, and every eagerly-satisfied figure (notably [ADR-064](#adr-064), which succeeds at apply time and never reaches the recruit) is **byte-identical** — the stability snapshot is green with **no `-u`**, zero happy-path drift. The flag means only: *its `t` is an unstated default (ADR-052), so it is RECRUITABLE on the failure path.* A stated ratio ("E on AC at 40%") gets no flag and stays fixed.
- **The recruit gains a "free the blocker" case (D)** in `recruitFreeDofs` ([step.ts](../src/engine/step.ts), the established failure-path-only pattern). When a just-failed constraint `K` reaches a drivable DOF `X` that an earlier constraint `K1` already CLAIMED, and `K1` references an `extension:true` on-segment `Y` not yet driving, the recruit **re-points `K1 → Y` and releases `X` to `K`** — so both keep a carrier and the joint solver redistributes (`AG⊥AD` drives `G`, freeing apex `A` for `∠ADB`). It runs in addition to the free-ancestor recruit (case B) and before the over-subscribed steal (case C). Because it fires only after `evaluate` already failed, an over-constraint with no such re-bind still fails honestly.

**Why this is the safe slice of R7 step-3 (and what's still deferred).** It does NOT retire the greedy `driveOrCheck` into one up-front joint decision, and it does NOT add a real per-DOF stay-put penalty (the existing `λ=1e-3` regulariser proved sufficient for these figures — `∠ADB` reaches 25°/30°/40° cleanly with AG⊥AD and D-B-G collinearity holding). Those two remain the genuinely larger, riskier rework (touching the hot path for every figure, gated by the `maxDelta` snapshot). This amendment unjams the reported class — a blocking greedy claim that a recruitable extension DOF can relieve — at the lowest risk, per the CLAUDE.md "narrow fix when the general one is larger, say so" rule. **Still deferred:** the up-front joint binding + real per-DOF stay-put penalty.

**Amendment (2026-06-21, hardening Pillar 4a) — the extension point is now SAMPLED (full ADR-052).** An `extension:true` on-segment point's distance beyond the segment is unstated, so "show another configuration" now varies it (`isSamplableExtension` in [sample.ts](../src/engine/sample.ts): sampled in `applySeed` to a `t ∈ [1.15, 2.2]`, kept past the far end, and listed in `freeDofs`). This closes the ADR-052 conformance smell it had been an example of — a DOF counted by `rawMovableDof` but absent from `freeDofs` (a default masquerading as fixed). It stays NOT eagerly driven (the ADR-074 distinction): driven only on the failure path, but freely sampled like any other unstated magnitude. At seed 0 it holds its default `t=1.3` (so the ADR-064 / bagrut scenarios are unaffected); a recruited (driven) extension point is excluded from sampling. Locked in `free-defaults.test.ts`.

**Amendment 2 (2026-06-21, hardening Pillar 4c) — "free the blocker" GENERALISED to any free DOF; fixes the equilateral / AAS false over-constraint.** An evidence-first probe for R7 binding fragility (before any speculative rework) found two REAL, plausible-student-input figures the greedy bind + recruit FALSELY rejected: **`triangle` + three stated sides** (an equilateral / scalene triangle) and **`triangle` + two angles + a side** (AAS/ASA) — both fully determined and constructible, yet reported `over-constrained`. Root cause is the same blocking pattern as ADR-074: greedy `driveOrCheck` claims two vertices for the first two constraints, the third finds both its refs claimed and the lone free vertex unreachable, and the recruit's narrow case (D) only re-bound to *extension* points. **Fix:** case (D)'s alternative-carrier finder is generalised from "an extension on-segment" to **`recruitableFreeDof`** — any unclaimed, movable, non-`line` carrier (a free VERTEX, an extension/free on-segment, a free on-circle, a shape scalar), still excluding pinned points and stated-ratio positions (ADR-064). So `|AB|=5` re-binds to vertex A, freeing B for `|BC|=5`, and the joint solver places the equilateral/AAS triangle. Still failure-path-only ⇒ the stability snapshot is green with **no `-u`** (zero happy-path drift); the genuine contradiction (square side 5 with diagonal 10) is still correctly rejected. Locked as `sweep-verify.test.ts` (equilateral / scalene / AAS). **Deliberately NOT done (no current evidence it's needed):** the full R7(3) "retire greedy `driveOrCheck` into one up-front joint decision + a real per-DOF stay-put penalty." The probe found no over-recruit DRIFT case (the stay-put penalty's purpose), and the failure-path re-bind covers the binding-fragility cases that actually fail — so per the audit ("deliberate, unhurried, gate it") and CLAUDE.md rule 4, that hot-path-for-every-figure rework stays a future dedicated session, not blocking "fully working + tested".

**Locked.** `scenarios.test.ts` `bagrut-q4-numeric-angle-drives-the-figure` (the operator's exact 7-step sequence with `∠ADB=30`, asserting the angle holds + AG⊥AD + D,B,G collinear + C,D on the circle and on line AO); the symbolic `bagrut-q4-tangent-secant-perpendicular` and `symbolic-2alpha-drives-shape-not-the-fixed-point` (ADR-064, D stays at t≈1.3) both stay green. Diagnosed via the Opus-oracle path (no live API).

## ADR-075 — "AB tangent at F" on an EXISTING line is a tangency CONSTRAINT that flexes the circle, not a new drawn tangent

**Status:** Accepted (2026-06-21) · built · 1187 tests green, build clean. A root-cause parser fix in the spirit of [ADR-052](#adr-052) (the circle's shape is a free DOF — flex it to meet a stated relation, like [ADR-070](#adr-070) flexed a rectangle for a concyclic given).

**The failure (operator session `ze5qda8y`).** Right-triangle `ABC`; `F,G,H` on sides `AB,AC,CB`; quad `GCHF` inscribed in a circle (`circumcircle` of `G,C,H` + `F` concyclic). Then `AB משיק למעגל בנקודה F` ("AB is tangent to the circle at F") **errored** `unresolved dependencies for: A, B, F, G, H, O, tan-F, circle-O`, and the LLM fallback then built nothing — so the circle was never adapted.

**Diagnosis (two layers).**

1. **The parser ran the wrong operation.** The `tangentLine` rule ([parse.ts](../src/parser/parse.ts)) treated "AB" as the *name of a fresh tangent line to be drawn* and emitted (confirmed in the log) `{tangent tan-F}` + `point-on-line A on tan-F` + `point-on-line B on tan-F` — **redefining the triangle's existing vertices `A,B` as markers on the new tangent.** That closes a dependency cycle `A → tan-F → circle-O → O = circumcentre(G,C,H) → G (on AC) → A`, which `evaluate` can't topologically order → "unresolved dependencies" → keep-prior → the step never builds.
2. **No constraint path existed.** A `tangent` is only ever a *line derived FROM the circle* (`LineSpec via:'tangent'`) — scaffolding, never a constraint on the circle. There was no way to read "the EXISTING line AB is tangent to the circle at F" as a relation that *adapts the circle*. Tangency at `F` (with `F` already on the circle) ⟺ **radius `O–F` ⟂ `AB`** — exactly the existing `set-perpendicular` constraint, which `driveOrCheck` flexes via a free DOF (here the `t` of `G/H` along their sides moving the circumcircle, and/or the triangle shape).

**Decision.** In `tangentLine`, discriminate by whether the line's naming labels are **pre-existing** points:

- **The labels and the touch point all pre-exist** (`AB`/`F` already placed) → emit a tangency **constraint** `set-perpendicular(centre, T, L1, L2)` and **nothing else** — no `tangent` object, no `point-on-line`. No new object ⇒ no cycle; the constraint references only existing points and flexes the circle so `O–F ⟂ AB` (and `F` stays on the circle via its existing concyclic incidence).
- **New labels naming a fresh drawn tangent** ("line CD tangent…", `C,D` new) → unchanged: draw the tangent + add the new labels as ±offset markers (ADR-036). Also hardened: a pre-existing label is now *skipped* when adding markers, so the cycle can't recur from a mixed naming either.

**Scope (narrow, per CLAUDE.md rule 4).** The constraint path assumes the touch point `T` is **already on the circle** (the marked-touch-point case — the only sensible reading of "AB tangent at F" where `F` is a labelled figure point). It does NOT additionally force `T` onto the circle, because (a) `circleMembers` reports only *structural* membership, so a concyclic-constrained `F` isn't detectable there, and (b) there is no clean single constraint to put an already-defined (on-segment) point onto a circle without redefining it. A tangency point not yet on the circle is a follow-up. The fix is correct and verified for the reported (and most common) case.

**Verified.** The operator's exact figure now builds with `lastError:null`, all steps `ok`, and the givens verifier ([ADR-053](#adr-053)) clean (`violations: []`): `cos∠(OF, AB) ≈ −3.5e-8` (radius ⟂ tangent) and `|OF| = |OG| = |OH| = |OC|` to 1e-7 (`F` on the circle). **Locked:** `scenarios.test.ts` `existing-line-tangent-adapts-the-circle` (the exact 4-step sequence) + `tangent-points.test.ts` (the rule emits `set-perpendicular` and no `point-on-line`; the new-label drawn-tangent path is unchanged). Diagnosed from `logs/debug-log.jsonl`; geometry validated via the Opus-oracle path (no live API).

## ADR-076 — "F, G, H on AB, AC, CB" — N points placed PAIRWISE on N segments is a deterministic parse (not an LLM escalation)

**Status:** Accepted (2026-06-21) · built · 1196 tests green, build clean. A parser-coverage fix found while validating ADR-075 (same figure). In the spirit of [ADR-066](#adr-066)'s lesson — extending the deterministic vocabulary is more robust than leaning on the LLM, and it also extends what the LLM can compose.

**The failure (operator session `svjp9x5e`).** Right-triangle `ABC`, then `נקודות F, G, H נמצאות על הישרים AB, AC, CB` ("points F, G, H are on the lines AB, AC, CB") **escalated to the LLM and built nothing**. The same structural utterance with `הצלעות` ("sides") had, in an earlier session, happened to get three `point-on-segment` commands from the LLM — so the figure-defining step's success depended on the live LLM's wording sensitivity (`הישרים` vs `הצלעות`), which is exactly the nondeterminism the parser-first design exists to avoid.

**Diagnosis.** The deterministic grammar had a single-point rule (`pointOnSegment`, "F on AB") and a two-points-on-ONE-segment rule (`pointsOnSegment`, "L and K on AC"), but **no rule for a LIST of points mapped pairwise to a LIST of segments** ("F, G, H on AB, AC, CB" → F on AB, G on AC, H on CB). So this very common figure-setup phrasing always escalated.

**Decision.** Add `pointsOnSegments`: split the utterance at the first "on"/"על"; take the **uppercase** point labels before it and the uppercase endpoint labels after it; require the right side to carry **exactly 2·N labels** for N points (≥2), chunked into pairs in order; emit `point-on-segment(P_i, a_i, b_i)`. Uppercase-only label extraction (the geometry convention, mirroring `tangentFromExternal`) means lowercase words ("points", "on", "sides", "lines") aren't mistaken for labels — so both `הצלעות` and `הישרים`, He and En, parse identically. A triangle "side"/"line" AB is the segment through A,B, so the natural representation is `point-on-segment`. Runs before `pointsOnSegment`/`pointOnSegment`; the `2·N` count guard makes it mutually exclusive with them (2-points-1-segment has N·2 = 2 ≠ labels for N=2, the singular has <2 points), and it defers circle phrasings. Added to the catalog (coverage map) so the in-app commands panel lists it.

**Verified.** All five wordings (He `הישרים`/`הצלעות`, with/without `נמצאות`; En "sides"/"lines") parse to the three commands; the two-on-one and singular cases are untouched. The full reported figure (right-triangle → F,G,H on the sides → inscribed quad → AB tangent at F, ADR-075) now builds end-to-end through the **deterministic** parser — no LLM step. **Locked:** `points-on-segments.test.ts` (the five phrasings + the non-steal guards) and the upgraded `scenarios.test.ts` `existing-line-tangent-adapts-the-circle` (its F,G,H step is now the real utterance, not an LLM mock). Diagnosed from `logs/debug-log.jsonl`.

## ADR-077 — "the diameter from F cuts side AC at E": a side-target intersection lands ON the segment (the figure flexes), and "קוטר … cuts …" parses

**Status:** Accepted (2026-06-21) · built · 1201 tests green, build clean. Same figure as ADR-075/076. Combines a parser-coverage fix with the [ADR-052](#adr-052)/[ADR-070](#adr-070) principle — when a stated relation can't hold at the current drawing, FLEX a free DOF rather than draw something that violates it.

**The failure (operator session `59tzde4c`).** After building the figure (right-triangle, F/G/H on the sides, inscribed quad GCHF, AB tangent at F), the operator typed `קוטר המעגל היוצא מנקודה F חותך את הצלע AC בנקודה E` ("the diameter of the circle from F cuts side AC at E"). **Two problems:** (1) it **escalated to the LLM and built nothing** — `lineLineIntersection` `stop`s the parse on the keyword "קוטר" (so the diameter/chord operands get created by the LLM, ADR-024), and there was no diameter-cuts-a-side rule; (2) the operator's manual workaround (`FO`, then `המשך FO חותך את הצלע AC בנקודה E` → a line∩line of FO with AC) put **E on the *continuation* of AC, not the segment** (measured `s ≈ 1.148` along AC, just past `C`). The operator: "I wanted E on AC, not its continuation — this would require point F to move."

**Diagnosis.** `line-line-intersection` finds where the two *infinite* lines cross; nothing constrains the crossing to lie within the named segment. Its `dir1`/`dir2` flags add only *beyond*-style order ([F,O,E] / [A,C,E]) — never the *between* order [A,E,C] that "the **side** AC" implies. So "the diameter cuts **the side** AC" was being read as "the diameter cuts **the line** AC", and the figure had no reason to flex even though it had the freedom (free DOFs `[A,C,B,H]` — F moves indirectly via the triangle).

**Decision.** Add `diameterCutsSegment` (before `lineLineIntersection`): the diameter through an on-circle point F is the line **F–O** (O the centre), so emit `line-line-intersection(E, F, O, X, Y, dir1)` for the crossing with line XY; and **when the target is a *side*/segment** (`הצלע`/"side"/"segment", not `הישר`/"line"), also emit **`set-line [X, E, Y]`** — a soft order constraint (E between X and Y) that drives a free DOF so the figure reshapes to bring E onto the segment instead of dropping it off the side. Draw the diameter chord F–E. The "side ⇒ on the segment, line ⇒ anywhere on the line" distinction is carried by the operand keyword.

**Verified.** `קוטר המעגל היוצא מנקודה F חותך את הצלע AC בנקודה E` now parses deterministically (no LLM); the figure flexes (A/F move) so **E lands at `s ≈ 0.879` on segment AC**, E stays collinear with F,O (on the diameter), `lastError:null`, givens verifier clean. Targeting "the **line** AC" omits the order constraint (E free on the line); a bare "diameter AB" is unchanged (the plain `diameter` rule). **Locked:** `scenarios.test.ts` `diameter-from-point-cuts-side-onto-segment` (the operator's intended 5-step sequence, asserting E on the segment + on the diameter) + `diameter-cuts-segment.test.ts` (He/En parse, side-vs-line, bare-diameter untouched); catalog entry added. **Scope / follow-up:** the side→segment-order fix lives only in this new rule; the *general* cut rules (`lineLineIntersection`, the perpendicular/tangent cut compounds) still read a "הצלע XY" target as the infinite line — generalising "side ⇒ keep the crossing on the segment" across them is a separate, broader change (noted, not done). Diagnosed from `logs/debug-log.jsonl`.

**Amendment (2026-06-21, operator session `50w3vlt3`) — a BARE "AC" defaults to the segment, not just an explicit "הצלע".** The first cut only added the `set-line [X,E,Y]` order constraint when the target carried "הצלע"/"side"/"segment" explicitly. But the operator naturally wrote `קוטר המעגל מנקודה F חותך את AC בנקודה E` — a *bare* "AC", no "הצלע" — and E went back to the extension ("still not working"). The operator then stated the governing principle: **"when the user writes a segment like 'AC' the meaning is the segment between A and C, not the continuation — unless the user says `חותך את המשך הצלע`."** So a bare "AC" (or "side AC") denotes the **segment/edge**, and the infinite line / the extension are the *marked* exceptions. **Fix:** constrain E to the segment **by default**, with two keyword opt-outs on the operand: (1) **"המשך AC" / "the extension of AC"** → E BEYOND the segment (order `[X,Y,E]` via the intersection's `dir2`, the ADR-054 directional convention — beyond the 2nd letter), no segment constraint; (2) **"הישר AC" / "the LINE AC"** → E free anywhere on the infinite line. So `…חותך את AC…` and `…חותך את הצלע AC…` keep E on the side and flex the figure; `…חותך את המשך AC…` puts E past it; `…חותך את הישר AC…` leaves E unconstrained on the line. Verified end-to-end on the operator's exact bare-"AC" wording (E at `s ≈ 0.879`, givens clean); the scenario + unit tests cover the bare, "הצלע", "המשך", and "הישר" forms. **Note:** this segment-default-with-`המשך`-opt-out is a *general* reading principle for segment references in cuts; it is currently implemented only in `diameterCutsSegment`. Extending it to the general intersection rules (`lineLineIntersection` cut-form, the perpendicular/tangent cut compounds) is the natural follow-up but touches central rules used by many scenarios (e.g. "EC חותך את AD בנקודה G"), so it needs its own carefully-regressed change — flagged, not yet done.

## ADR-078 — on-circle resample jitter is scoped to genuine inscribed polygons, so a chord + secant figure draws cleanly

**Status:** Accepted (2026-06-21, operator session `m26xv4m2`).

**Context.** Q4 (bagrut) — quadrilateral BKCD with KB ∥ CD, CD a chord, KB tangent at K, and side BD a secant cutting the circle at A. It is encoded with the tangent–chord theorem (KB tangent at K **and** KB ∥ CD ⟺ **K is the arc-midpoint of CD**), then **B = (tangent at K) ∩ (line AD)**, A a free on-circle point, E = AK ∩ BC. The construction is geometrically exact (verified: KB ∥ CD, OK ⊥ KB, B–A–D collinear, K/A/C/D concyclic, `lastError:null`). But the operator could not get it to **draw** like the textbook: the clean figure needs a **convex** trapezoid BKCD **and** A strictly **between** B and D (the secant "BD cuts the circle at A"). Brute-forcing 300 seeds × both arc branches, only the arc-midpoint's branch 1 gives "A between", and a convex-AND-A-between drawing occurred in **1 seed of 300** — while "show another configuration" only searches **24** seeds, so it essentially never landed it.

**Root cause.** `sample.ts` `applySeed` set the on-circle jitter width from the **total count** of free on-circle points: `≤2 → ±153° (wide)`, `≥3 → ±30° (tight)`, to stop an inscribed *polygon* collapsing to a sliver. In Q4 the chord ends (C, D) and the lone secant point A are **three** free on-circle points but **not one inscribed cluster** — yet they read as "3 → a polygon" and were pinned tight, so the chord CD could never get short enough to spread K, A, B into the clean trapezoid.

**Decision.** Decide the jitter width **per point** by structural grouping, not raw count. A free on-circle point gets the **tight** ±30° (protect spread) iff it is a vertex of a **genuine inscribed polygon** — a declared `polygon` with **≥3 free-on-circle vertices** — **or**, as a legacy fallback, the figure declares **no** polygon touching the circle at all (no grouping signal to trust) and there are ≥3 free on-circle points. Otherwise it ranges **wide** (±153°). In Q4, trapezoid BKCD has only **2** on-circle vertices (C, D; B is derived, K is the arc-midpoint), and a polygon *does* touch the circle, so the fallback doesn't fire → C, D, A all range wide, and the chord shortens freely.

**Result.** Measured over 300 seeds on the correct arc (branch 1): convex-AND-A-between rose from **~0.3 % → ~30 %** (≈100×), first clean config at **seed 7** — so "show another configuration" (which cycles the arc branch **and** reseeds) now reaches the textbook drawing in a few clicks. Real inscribed triangles/quads are untouched (they declare a `poly-…` with ≥3 on-circle vertices → still tight), and a *bare* triple of on-circle points (no polygon) keeps the old spread protection via the fallback.

**Verified.** 1205 tests green, build clean. Locked by `alt-config.test.ts` → "Q4 tangent/secant trapezoid" (the construction builds with every step ok; within 24 seeds, branch 1 yields a convex BKCD with A strictly between B and D), plus the unchanged `phase-sample.test.ts` spread-protection and `alt-config` inscribed-triangle/-square cases. Diagnosed from `logs/debug-log.jsonl`.

**Scope / follow-up.** This makes the figure **reliably reachable**, not deterministic-on-first-draw: the parser defaults the arc-midpoint to branch 0 (A beyond D) and `resample` accepts the first convex *different* drawing without checking the secant order, so a few "show another configuration" presses may be needed. A fully deterministic render would require either constraining A onto segment BD (deriving A as the near secant crossing — needs a "point on the tangent line" construct) or a trapezoid-first construction; both were considered and deferred in favour of the smaller, generic seeder fix (operator's call).

## ADR-079 — the parser auto-materialises an IMPLICIT circle referenced before it is created

**Status:** Accepted (2026-06-21, operator session `m26xv4m2`).

**Context.** Live-testing the LLM fallback on the verbatim Q4 problem (operator authorised one Haiku call), Haiku decomposed it in **reading order** and — crucially — treated the circle as *given*: it emitted "מיתר CD במעגל O" / "KB משיק למעגל O בנקודה K" / "הישר BD חותך את מעגל O בנקודה A" but **no step that creates circle O** (the problem says "CD is a chord *in the circle*" — the circle is implicit, never stated as an object). The engine left centre `O` undefined, so the tangent (needs `O`) and the secant cascaded to **"unresolved dependencies"** and the whole figure collapsed. This is the [ADR-073](#adr-073) "insert implicit objects" gap, hit in practice: an LLM naturally omits "create the circle" because the prose presents it as given.

**Decision.** In the PARSER (`withImplicitCircles`, applied to every rule's output in `parse`), when a command **consumes** a circle (`point-on-circle` / `tangent` / `arc-midpoint` / `line-circle-intersection` / `diameter` / `extend-onto-circle`) whose `circle-<centre>` is neither in the figure context (`ctx.circles`) nor defined by the same utterance, **prepend a free circle** (`{type:'circle', freeRadius:true, ifAbsent:true}` — free centre via `placeBase`, free radius per [ADR-052](#adr-052), since the radius is unstated). The circle-DEFINING commands are excluded (they make their own). It lives in the parser — interpreting natural input — so the **engine stays strict**: a referenced-but-*removed* circle still cascades honestly (explicit removal is never silently rescued — the phase-3 "no phantom rescue" invariant holds).

**The `ifAbsent` flag.** The injected circle carries `ifAbsent:true`; `applyCommand`'s `circle` case **skips it when that circle already exists**, so it never CLOBBERS a real circle with the default free radius. This matters because a later consuming step parsed *without* the circle in context (e.g. the standalone `parse(u)` used by many engine tests, or a context-threading slip) would otherwise inject a fresh radius-5 circle over a real radius-4 one. With `ifAbsent`, only the **first** truly-absent reference materialises the circle; every later reference is a harmless no-op. Decouples correctness from perfect context threading.

**Result.** The exact live-Haiku Q4 decomposition now builds (`lastError:null`, no unresolved dependencies; circle O auto-created). The remaining `C not on circle` is the SEPARATE trapezoid-first convergence issue (the reading-order build is still geometrically fragile — that's the deeper [ADR-073](#adr-073) reorder/repair work), correctly flagged amber by the givens verifier — not a silent-wrong. **1209 tests green, build clean.** Locked by `implicit-circle.test.ts` (injection present/absent by context, He/En, the no-clobber multi-step replay) + the unchanged phase-3 strict-removal and adr028-driven / phase5c-Q6 radius tests. Diagnosed from `logs/debug-log.jsonl` + the live fallback call.

## ADR-080 — declaring an EXISTING free vertex "on circle O" makes it a point ON the circle

**Status:** Accepted (2026-06-21, operator session `m26xv4m2`).

**Context.** After [ADR-079](#adr-079) auto-creates an implicit circle, the live-Haiku Q4 build still left **C not on the circle** ("C should lie on circle O … but is 7.07 from its centre" — verifier amber). Root cause in `applyCommand`'s `point-on-circle` case: when the named point ALREADY EXISTS as a free vertex (a `quadrilateral` corner) and the circle's radius is **free** (not pinned by a through-point), every reconciliation branch fell through to case (d) — "can't reconcile structurally; the verifier reports it" — so **no constraint was added** and the vertex stayed adrift. Case (c) only handled a `through`-radius circle (grow the circle to a fixed vertex); a free-radius circle had no path, so "C,D on circle O" silently failed to make CD a chord.

**Decision.** Add case (c2): when the existing point is a **non-pinned free vertex** and the circle is not a `through`-radius circle, **convert the free point to an on-circle point** (`{kind:'on-circle', free:true, theta:nextTheta(…)}`). It then lies on the circle by construction (0 residual), and the joint solver places its angle together with the free centre/radius and the other free DOFs. This is the "shapes carry their true DOF" model ([ADR-033](#adr-033)): declaring a quad's C,D "on circle O" makes CD a real chord rather than leaving the corners off the circle. A **pinned** point (explicitly placed by the student — a given, not a DOF) is excluded by the guard: it is not slid onto the circle, so case (d) still reports honestly. The `through`-radius case (c) is unchanged (there the CIRCLE grows to the fixed vertex — ADR-057 A1).

**Result.** "quad BKCD" + "C,D on circle O" now lands C,D exactly on the circle (equal radius), no violation. **1211 tests green, build clean.** Locked by `free-point-on-circle.test.ts` (conversion + the pinned-point guard) and the rewritten `implicit-circle.test.ts` (the materialised circle is REAL — C,D become a genuine chord).

**Scope.** This fixes the *relation* generically. It does NOT make the whole live-Haiku reading-order Q4 decomposition correct: that decomposition applies `KB∥CD` to a free quad and THEN makes C,D a chord, which over-constrains **trapezoid-first** ("KB ∥ CD cannot hold") — and its tangent step builds a tangent to a fresh point T rather than asserting KB-tangent-at-K. Those are the deeper reorder/decomposition-quality problems owned by [ADR-073](#adr-073) (whole-problem LLM reorder + verifier-gated repair); with the on-circle relation now real, the flawed order fails **honestly** (an over-constraint) instead of silently drawing C off the circle.

## ADR-081 — "KB tangent at K": a segment tangent at its OWN endpoint is a tangency relation, not a tangent-from-external

**Status:** Accepted (2026-06-22, operator session `m26xv4m2`).

**Context.** "KB tangent to circle O at K" (the Q4 side KB tangent at K, K an endpoint of KB) silently did the wrong thing: the parser matched `tangentFromExternal` (which runs before `tangentLine`), read **B** as an external apex, **invented a new touch point T** (the Thales circle on O–B ∩ circle O), drew the tangent **B–T**, and **ignored K entirely** — both the "at K" and the K in "KB". So KB was never made tangent; K stayed an unrelated free vertex; and because nothing *asserted* "KB tangent at K", the givens verifier had nothing to flag (silently wrong). Two compounding causes: (1) `tangentFromExternal` greedily claimed it even though the named touch K already existed (its construction computes the touch itself, so it can't honour a given one); (2) `tangentLine`'s ADR-075 path couldn't catch it either — its `naming` guard *excludes the touch point from the line labels*, and here the touch K **is** an endpoint of the named segment KB, so `naming` nulled out.

**Decision.** Two scoped parser changes:
- `tangentFromExternal` **defers** (returns null) when the named "at X" touch point already exists (`have.has(atPoint)`) — that is a tangent-*at* an existing point, not a tangent-*from* an external apex.
- `tangentLine` adds a case: when the named segment's two labels both exist and **include the touch point T as an endpoint**, emit **`point-on-circle T` + `set-perpendicular(O, T, P, Q)`** (T on the circle; radius O–T ⟂ the segment PQ). The figure then flexes so the segment is a real tangent at its endpoint. (ADR-075's separate-touch case — "line CD tangent at F", F not on CD — is unchanged.)

**Result.** "KB tangent at K" (He/En, with/without "הצלע") now emits `point-on-circle K` + `set-perpendicular(O,K,K,B)`; a clean build lands K on the circle with OK ⟂ KB (verified, no violations). **1213 tests green, build clean.** Locked by `tangent-points.test.ts` (routing for the three phrasings — no invented touch / Thales scaffolding — plus an end-to-end build asserting K-on-circle and OK⟂KB); the full external-tangent suite is unaffected (those name a NEW/auto touch point, so the guard doesn't fire).

**Scope.** This fixes the tangent *phrasing* generically. It does NOT make the whole live-Haiku reading-order Q4 build succeed: that decomposition still applies `KB∥CD` to a free quad **before** the chord, which over-constrains trapezoid-first ("KB ∥ CD cannot hold") and cascades to the secant — the deeper reorder problem owned by [ADR-073](#adr-073). With this fix the tangent STEP itself now builds cleanly (was the bogus B–T construction).

## ADR-082 — "KB tangent to the circle" with NO explicit touch point: infer it from the on-circle endpoint

**Status:** Accepted (2026-06-22, operator sessions `xstllu0i` / `mmfbpvaz`).

**Context.** This is the SAME tangent reported broken "3rd or 4th time" — each prior fix ([ADR-075](#adr-075), [ADR-081](#adr-081)) handled a tangent phrased with an explicit touch point ("KB tangent **at K**" / "בנקודה K"), but the operator kept typing the natural form **without** it: "**KB משיק למעגל**" / "KB tangent to the circle". The debug log shows this exact step returning `not-understood` / `built-nothing` after the figure already had quad BKCD, KB∥CD, and triangle KCD inscribed in circle O (so K is on the circle). The point was never that KB *cannot* be tangent — fed the command the engine builds it perfectly (K on circle, ∠OKB = 90°, KB∥CD, **verifier clean**). The point was that the natural utterance never reached the engine.

**Root cause (parser, not engine).** `tangentLine` derived the touch point T **only** from an explicit `at`/`בנקודה` clause and bailed at the top otherwise: `if (!center || !atM) return null`. So the entire ADR-075/081 endpoint-tangency logic sat behind a gate the natural phrasing never opened. `tangentFromExternal` couldn't catch it either: both K and B already exist as quad vertices, so there is no *unique* external apex (its existing-point heuristic needs exactly one) and it returned null. Nothing handled it → LLM → "not-understood". The defect was a **missing inference**: a tangent whose touch point is an endpoint *already on the circle* needs no naming — geometrically the touch can only be that endpoint.

**Decision.** In `tangentLine`, when there is no `at` clause, **infer T from the named segment's endpoint that is a member of THIS circle** (via `ctx.circleMembers`). Guard: **exactly one** endpoint may be on the circle — both on it would make the segment a chord (crosses at two points), not a tangent, so we do not infer. With T inferred, the existing [ADR-081](#adr-081) branch fires unchanged, emitting `point-on-circle T` + `set-perpendicular(O, T, P, Q)`. Purely additive: when an `at` clause is present the behaviour is byte-identical to before; when it is absent and no endpoint is on the circle, the rule still returns null (a genuine external tangent stays with `tangentFromExternal`, or escalates) — no regression.

**Result.** The exact failing utterance "KB משיק למעגל" (and "KB tangent to the circle") now parses and builds a true tangent end-to-end: K on the circle (|OK|=|OC|=|OD|), OK ⟂ KB, KB ∥ CD, **zero verifier violations**. **1214 tests green, build clean.** Locked by a real end-to-end regression scenario — the operator's exact 4-utterance sequence — in `scenarios.test.ts` (`segment-tangent-no-explicit-touch-point`), replayed through the live parse-with-context → fact list → `replay` path. Diagnosed from `logs/debug-log.jsonl`.

**Why the earlier fixes kept "missing."** Each addressed a real but *different* sub-phrasing of the same figure (separate touch F on a line — ADR-075; touch named at an existing endpoint — ADR-081), and each was unit-true. None covered the touch-point-**omitted** case, which is the form a student actually types when the touch is obvious. Lesson reinforced (CLAUDE.md "root cause, never patch"): a green unit test on a phrasing variant is **not** proof the *feature* — "a segment tangent to a circle" — works; the operator's verbatim utterance through the real pipeline is the bar.

## ADR-083 — "one circle → no name": a definite "the circle" / "המעגל" resolves to the single circle

**Status:** Accepted (2026-06-22, operator principle).

**Context.** Operator: *"when there is only ONE circle in the diagram, I should not have to say its name."* Surfaced while completing the full bagrut Q4 (after the [ADR-082](#adr-082) tangent fix): the secant step **"BD חותך את המעגל בנקודה A"** — "BD cuts **the** circle at A", definite article, no name — returned `not-handled` and escalated to the LLM, even though the figure held exactly one circle.

**Root cause.** The single-crossing circle-intersection rules `lineMeetsCircle` and `extendOntoCircle` required a NAMED circle in **two** places: (1) the centre came from `circleCenter(s)` (named-only) — they deliberately avoided `resolveCenter`'s single-circle fallback so a plain line∩line / point-on-extension isn't misread as a circle cut; and (2) the crossing-point anchor `(?:circle|מעגל)\s+[A-Za-z]\d*` demanded a **name letter** after the circle word to locate the "at X" that follows the circle mention. Both fail on "the circle" / "המעגל" (no name letter; in Hebrew "מעגל" is followed by "בנקודה", not a label). So the natural definite form never matched.

**Decision.** Honour the principle **without** re-opening the line∩line false-grab. Two shared helpers in the parser:
- `resolveMentionedCircle(s, ctx)` — the named centre, or the single circle **only when the utterance mentions a circle at all** (`/circle|מעגל/`, i.e. named *or* the definite "the circle"). Unlike `resolveCenter`, it does **not** grab the single circle for an utterance that never says "circle" — so a bare "AC cuts BD at E" stays a line∩line.
- `crossingAfterCircle(s)` — finds the "at X" / "בנקודה X" that follows the circle word, with the **name optional** (`(?:circle|מעגל)(?:\s+[A-Za-z]\d*)?`), so the definite form anchors too.

Applied to `lineMeetsCircle` and `extendOntoCircle` (the only two rules that bailed on a missing name; the chord, tangent, two-crossing-secant, and external-tangent rules already fell back to the single circle). Scope-limited on purpose: a rule gated only on a generic keyword (here `INTERSECT_KW`) must see an explicit circle mention before it claims the single circle.

**Result.** "BD חותך את המעגל בנקודה A" (He/En, unnamed) now parses and builds — A lands on the circle, the [ADR-082](#adr-082) tangent and KB∥CD givens still hold, **verifier clean** — so the full Q4 completes deterministically. A plain "AC חותך את BD בנקודה E" with one circle present is **still** a `line-line-intersection`, not a chord (no false grab). Named circles are unchanged. **1219 tests green, build clean.** Locked by `unnamed-circle.test.ts` (positive definite/named + the NEGATIVE line∩line guard, He/En) and the end-to-end `unnamed-circle-secant-full-q4` scenario.

## ADR-084 — a tangent's named external point appears immediately, then a later fact drives it to the crossing

**Status:** Accepted (2026-06-22, operator session `nhm9154u` / `twiwst5h`).

**Context.** Triangle ABC inscribed in circle O, then the operator built a tangent in the natural two-step way:
1. "מנקודה D יוצא משיק למעגל בנקודה B" — *from point D* a tangent touches at B.
2. "המשך CA נפגש עם המשיק בנקודה D" — the *extension of CA* meets *the tangent* at D.

Step 1 drew the tangent at B but **dropped D** (operator: "still didnt create point D and just created a tangent line"); step 2 was `not-handled` → LLM → built nothing. The engine was never the obstacle — the **one-sentence** form ("המשיק בנקודה B והמשך CA נפגשים בנקודה D") already builds D as a `line-intersection` of the tangent and line CA. The gap was purely parsing the **split, accumulating** phrasing (the tool's core interaction model, FR-EN-7).

**Decision (operator's call: D appears at step 2).** Two scoped parser changes, no engine change and **no parse-context plumbing**:
- `tangentLine` now creates the **named external apex** ("from D" / "מנקודה D …") as a free **`point-on-line` marker on the tangent** when it is new and ≠ the touch point. So D appears immediately and slides along the tangent (a genuine 1-DOF, [ADR-052](#adr-052)).
- A tight new rule `extensionMeetsExistingPoint` reads "the extension of CA meets the tangent at **D**" (D **existing**) as **`set-line [C, A, D]`** — D is *already* on the tangent, so the only new information is "D on the CA extension", and that **drives D's on-line DOF to the crossing**, in order C→A→D (the directional `המשך`, [ADR-054](#adr-054)). It needs no reference to the tangent line's id — which is exactly why no context plumbing is required.

**Why no tangent-line id in context.** Because step 1 puts D *on* the tangent, step 2 never has to name the tangent: constraining D to the CA extension intersects the two automatically. The rule is scoped to avoid mis-grabbing a plain line∩line cut-form: it fires only on an extension word + a tangent/line object (not a second segment) + a "meets/at D" where **D already exists** (a new crossing is left to the intersection constructs) + **no circle mention** ([ADR-083](#adr-083) owns the circle case).

**Result.** The operator's exact three utterances now build end-to-end: after step 2 **D exists** on the tangent; after step 3 it is pinned with DB ⟂ OB (true tangent), D collinear with C,A, and D beyond A (the extension) — **verifier clean**. Proven first at the engine level (a `point-on-line` marker driven by `set-line` lands on the tangent∩CA crossing) before any parser rule was written. **1219 tests green, build clean** (no regressions — the apex marker and the new rule are both additive). Locked by the `tangent-from-external-D-then-pinned-by-extension` end-to-end scenario. Diagnosed from `logs/debug-log.jsonl`.

## ADR-085 — the default inscribed figure is generically scalene, and a lone on-line marker may flip sides

**Status:** Accepted (2026-06-22, operator session `vob7kih2`).

**Context.** After [ADR-084](#adr-084) the operator reported two things on the same figure (triangle ABC inscribed, tangent at B, D where the extension of CA meets it): (1) the one-sentence form "המשיק למעגל בנקודה B והמשך CA נפגשים בנקודה D" **errored** "cannot construct D: lines tan-B and line-CA are parallel"; (2) D always appeared on the **same side** of B across "show another configuration".

**Root cause — one flaw, two symptoms: fixed defaults ([ADR-052](#adr-052)).**
- On-circle points are placed by a golden-angle spread (`nextTheta`). For exactly 3 points that yields **two equal gaps** → an **isosceles** triangle whose apex B sits at the **arc-midpoint of AC**. Two consequences: it silently asserts AB=BC (a given the student never stated), and — by the tangent–chord relation — the **tangent at an arc-midpoint is exactly parallel to the chord**. So at the default seed tangent@B ∥ CA, and the deterministic `line-intersection` (tangent ∩ line CA) has no solution → the error. (Measured: seed 0 gave AB=BC=9.32, angle(tan@B, CA)=0.00°.) This is long-standing, not a regression from ADR-084 — but ADR-081 newly routes the tangent through the path that *surfaces* it instead of silently drawing a wrong tangent.
- The tangent's external apex marker (ADR-084) is a single `point-on-line` at a fixed `+offset`; the sampler scaled its magnitude but **preserved its sign** (a rule meant to keep a ±*pair* straddling the anchor), so a *lone* marker was stuck on one side.

**Decision.**
- **Scalene default:** add a small **bounded alternating skew** to `nextTheta` (`+ (n % 2) * SCALENE_SKEW`, `SCALENE_SKEW = 0.35`). It breaks the equal-gap symmetry so 3 points form a generic scalene triangle (tangent@B ∦ CA), and being bounded it does not accumulate over larger inscribed polygons. (Operator chose this general root fix over a localized per-construct workaround.)
- **Lone-marker sign:** in the sampler, when a line carries **only one** free on-line marker, let "show another configuration" **flip its sign** (either side of the anchor); a line with a **±pair** keeps the old sign-preserving behaviour so the pair keeps straddling.

**Result.** The default inscribed triangle is scalene (e.g. 9.81/8.55/6.75); the one-shot "tangent meets CA" builds at the default seed (D is the genuine tangent∩CA intersection); the split form still builds; and D now samples to **both** sides of B. **1220 tests green, build clean.** Fallout was exactly the 2 coordinate snapshots in `stability-snapshot.test.ts` (intended placement change → updated with `-u`) and the ADR-036 on-line marker test (updated to assert the new both-sides behaviour). Locked by `scalene-default.test.ts` (distinct sides + non-parallel tangent), the updated `phase-sample.test.ts` (lone marker → both sides), and the `inscribed-triangle-scalene-tangent-meets-CA` end-to-end scenario. Diagnosed from `logs/debug-log.jsonl`.

## ADR-086 — "C on the extension of DA" when C already exists drives it in ORDER (D→A→C), not to the near intersection

**Status:** Accepted (2026-06-22, operator session).

**Context.** Triangle ABC inscribed, tangent at B from D, then "C נמצאת על המשך DA" ("C is on the continuation of DA"). The operator wanted C **beyond A** (order D→A→C). It aligned D, A, C collinear but in the **wrong order** — C landed *between* D and A (parameter 0.18 along D→A), at the near secant intersection.

**Root cause.** `pointOnExtension` always emitted `point-on-segment {id, a, b, t: 1.3, extension: true}` — a *fresh* off-object point beyond the far end. But C **already existed** as an on-circle vertex (from the inscribed triangle). Re-issuing it as a new on-segment point did not move it off the circle; the apply path kept C on the circle and reduced the fact to a bare collinearity, which the solver satisfied at the **near** of the two line∩circle intersections, **dropping the requested order** (t=1.3 was meaningless for a point pinned to the circle).

**Decision.** In `pointOnExtension`, branch on whether the named point already exists (`ctx.points`):
- **New point** → unchanged: `point-on-segment … extension` (created beyond the far end).
- **Existing point** → emit an **ordered collinearity `set-line [a, b, id]`** (here `[D, A, C]`). The point is *driven*, on whatever carrier it already has, to lie collinear **and in order** a→b→id — so an on-circle C becomes the **far** secant point (beyond A), staying on the circle, in the order the student asked.

**Result.** "C נמצאת על המשך DA" now yields D(0) → A(1.0) → **C(1.22)** along D→A (C beyond A), collinear, C still on the circle (|OC|=|OA|). Proven at the engine level (`set-line [D,A,C]` gives the right order) before changing the parser. **1223 tests green, build clean**, no regressions (the new-point path is untouched). Locked by the `existing-vertex-on-extension-keeps-order` end-to-end scenario. Diagnosed from `logs/debug-log.jsonl`. Generalises the [ADR-050](#adr-050)/ADR-054 collinear-order family to the "X on the extension of YZ" phrasing when X pre-exists.

## ADR-087 — set a circle's radius BY VALUE (no segment, no invented point)

**Status:** Accepted (2026-06-22, operator session).

**Context.** Incircle P of a triangle, then "רדיוס מעגל P הוא 4" ("the radius of circle P is 4") → built-nothing (escalated to the LLM). To set the size the operator had to invent a point and write "PF=4" — which both forced a name (F) and **drew the radius segment**. There was no way to state an *existing* circle's radius by value: the `circle` rule only reads a radius at creation ("circle O radius 5"), and a constructed circle's size is determined by its construction.

**Decision.** Add a first-class **`set-radius {circle, value}`** command + a `setRadius` parser rule ("radius of circle P is 4" / "רדיוס מעגל P (הוא) 4" / "radius of P = 4", He/En). The engine resolves it per the circle's `RadiusSpec`:
- `via: 'through'` (an incircle's radius is |centre · its tangent foot|) → a **`distance(centre, point, value)` constraint** that *flexes the figure* to the stated size (the incircle stays the incircle; the triangle resizes — ADR-052).
- `via: 'free' | 'length'` → set the radius to `{via:'length', value}` (a stated size is a given).
- `via: 'tangent-inner'` → no own size to pin; left for the verifier to flag if it can't hold.

The rule fires **only for an EXISTING circle** (named, a bare centre-letter that is a known circle, or the single circle) — a fresh "circle O radius 5" still falls through to `circle` (creation). It runs before `circle` and the shape rules (which 'stop' on רדיוס).

**Result.** "רדיוס מעגל P הוא 4" now resolves the incircle's radius to exactly 4.000 with **no segment drawn and no point invented** (He + En). **1228 tests green, build clean.** Locked by the `set-circle-radius-by-value-no-segment` scenario. Proven engine-first (a distance constraint on centre·foot sets the radius) before the parser rule. Diagnosed from `logs/debug-log.jsonl`.

## ADR-088 — hide a circle (a display preference, like hiding a line/segment)

**Status:** Accepted (2026-06-22, operator session).

**Context.** Segments and point labels can be hidden on the canvas (click → context menu → hide, FR-RN-10), but **circles could not** — an auxiliary/construction circle always drew. The operator asked for "the ability to hide a circle in a similar way to what we have with a line."

**Decision.** Mirror the segment-hide exactly. Store: a `hiddenCircles: Id[]` **display set** + `toggleCircleHidden(id)` — UI-only, excluded from temporal history by `partialize`, rewritten by `rename`/`merge` (a hidden `circle-F` tracks to `circle-T`), reset by `clear`. Render: a hidden circle draws as a faint dashed **ghost** (still clickable, so the toggle is reversible) instead of the solid outline; circles get a wide transparent **hit-ring** so the outline is clickable, opening the existing on-canvas menu with a `circle` kind (operator chose click→menu over a text command). The engine is untouched — the circle still constrains its points; only the drawing is suppressed (distinct from the construction-level `Circle.hidden` used for a cyclic-quad's undrawn circle).

**Result.** Clicking a circle opens a menu with hide/show; a hidden circle vanishes (faint ghost) but its geometry holds. **1228 tests green, build clean.** Locked by `hidden.test.ts` (toggle / rename-rewrite / clear) + the unchanged phase-2 render tests; typecheck clean with the new `Figure` props.

## ADR-089 — a typo that makes a rule match PARTIALLY (dropping a new label) escalates to the LLM

**Status:** Accepted (2026-06-22, operator session).

**Context.** The operator typed "**מנוקדה** D יוצא משיק למעגל בנקודה B" — a typo of "מנקודה" (the ק/ו swapped). The deterministic parser still matched the tangent rule on the correctly-spelled tail ("…בנקודה B"), drew the tangent at B, but **silently dropped the "from D" apex** (the typo'd "מנוקדה D" wasn't recognised). It then **committed that partial figure** instead of escalating to the LLM — whose job is exactly freeform/typo input ([ADR-023](#adr-023); the operator's standing note: typo-tolerance is the LLM's role, not the grammar's). This is the [N1 escalation-calibration gap](PROJECT-MEMORY.md): a parser-handled-but-subtly-wrong parse never escalates.

**Decision.** Add a parser→LLM escalation **gate**: after a successful deterministic parse, if the utterance names a **NEW** uppercase point label (not already in the figure) that the emitted commands **never reference**, treat the parse as **weak** and escalate to the LLM rather than commit. The signal is `droppedNewLabels(utterance, commands, existingPoints)`: input labels minus (labels used anywhere in the commands, incl. inside ids like `circle-P`/`tan-B`) minus (labels already in the figure). Wired into `App.submit` alongside the existing dry-run gate.

**Why "new" and "unconsumed".** A typo'd keyword that drops a clause leaves the label it introduced **orphaned** — it appears in the text but in no command. Requiring the label to be **NEW** avoids a false escalation when a command merely doesn't re-reference an **existing** point (e.g. a tangent at an already-placed point — that label is context, not dropped). Validated across 12 real per-step sequences: **zero false positives**; the typo flags `["D"]`, the correctly-spelled twin flags `[]`.

**Result.** "מנוקדה D …" no longer commits a tangent-without-D; it escalates to the LLM (which normalises the typo and emits the full tangent + apex). The grammar stays strict (no fuzzy keyword matching — that long tail is the LLM's job). **1232 tests green, build clean.** Locked by `dropped-labels.test.ts` (the operator's exact typo flags D; the correct spelling and existing-label/​id-embedded cases flag nothing). The end-to-end LLM recovery is exercised in the app (the fallback is mocked in tests, per the no-autonomous-API rule).

## ADR-090 — a circle DEFINED BY its diameter AB (centre = midpoint of AB)

**Status:** Accepted (2026-06-22, operator session).

**Context.** After a triangle ABC, the operator tried 5+ ways to make a circle whose diameter is AB and **none worked**: the define phrasings ("AB קוטר של מעגל", "מעגל שבו AB קוטר", "circle with diameter AB") were **not-handled** (→ LLM, nothing), and "AB קוטר של מעגל O" **misrouted** to the `diameter` rule — which adds a diameter to an EXISTING circle by making one endpoint a free on-circle point and the other its antipode. With A,B already existing (triangle vertices) that re-creation **errored** ("…no longer available"). There was no construct for a circle *defined by* its diameter (the inverse of `diameter`).

**Decision.** Add a `circleOnDiameter` rule for the **define** phrasings — "of/with/whose diameter", He+En ("של מעגל" / "שקוטרו" / "מעגל שבו … קוטר" / "circle with diameter" / "is the diameter of"). It emits **segment AB → midpoint(centre) → circle-through(centre, A)**: the centre is the **midpoint of AB**, the radius is |AB|/2, and A,B are the diameter's endpoints (B on the circle by the midpoint relation). The segment both **creates A,B if new** (`midpoint` needs them) and draws the diameter. Works whether A,B are new or pre-existing; the centre is the named circle ("circle O" → O is the centre/midpoint) or auto.

**Distinguishing define from add.** The discriminator is the **phrasing**, not whether the circle exists: a positive define marker (of/with/whose-diameter) is required, so the **add** phrasing "diameter DE **in** circle O" / "קוטר DE **במעגל** O" (and the cyclic-quad "AD קוטר במעגל ABCD") is left to `diameter`. Runs before `diameter` and `circle`.

**Result.** All five define phrasings build a circle with AB as a true diameter (|OA|=|OB|, centre at the midpoint), He+En, new or existing A,B — including the operator's exact "triangle ABC → AB קוטר של מעגל O". The add-a-diameter path and the Q6 / adr028 / bagrut-4d diameter tests are unchanged. **1235 tests green, build clean.** Locked by `circle-on-diameter.test.ts` (define phrasings → segment/midpoint/circle-through; add phrasing → diameter) and the `circle-defined-by-its-diameter` scenario. Diagnosed from `logs/debug-log.jsonl`.

## ADR-091 — midpoint creates its endpoints; a circle defined by centre/radius + diameter

**Status:** Accepted (2026-06-22, operator session).

**Context.** Two failures the operator hit, both **standard constructs that escalated to the LLM (which built nothing)** when they should be handled deterministically:
1. "B אמצע הקטע AC" on an empty figure → `midpoint` → **"unresolved dependencies for: B"**. `midpoint` (unlike `segment`) did not create its endpoints, so with A,C absent there was nothing to bisect.
2. "AB קוטר במעגל שמרכזו O ורדיוסו R" (AB is a diameter in a circle whose centre is O, radius R) → routed to `diameter` (the "במעגל" = *in* a circle) → **"'B' is already defined"** (it tried to make the existing B an antipode), although the circle was being **defined** by its centre/radius.

These reinforce the [N1 calibration point](PROJECT-MEMORY.md): the LLM cannot rescue a STRUCTURAL gap (it would emit the same midpoint and hit the same wall), and core constructs should not depend on it anyway. The LLM *was* reached in both cases (the log shows `src:llm`) and built nothing.

**Decision.**
1. **`midpoint` creates its endpoints when new.** "B is the midpoint of segment AC" implies the segment AC: when an endpoint is not already in the figure, `midpoint` prepends an (idempotent) `segment` that creates + draws AC; when both endpoints exist it emits just the midpoint (unchanged — no surprise segment on existing figures).
2. **`circleOnDiameter`'s define-markers ([ADR-090](#adr-090)) now include a centre/radius specification** (שמרכזו / ורדיוסו / centered / radius). You describe a circle's centre/radius when **defining** it, so "AB קוטר **במעגל שמרכזו O ורדיוסו R**" is a define (centre O = midpoint of AB), not an add — while plain "diameter X **in** circle O" (no centre/radius clause) still routes to `diameter`. The label extraction strips the centre/radius clause so only the diameter's endpoints feed `labelRun`.

**Result.** "B אמצע הקטע AC" builds B at the midpoint of a freshly-created AC; "AB קוטר במעגל שמרכזו O ורדיוסו R" builds a circle centred at the midpoint of AB (|OA|=|OB|), no "already defined" error. The add-a-diameter path (incl. the cyclic-quad "AD קוטר במעגל ABCD") is unchanged. **1235 tests green, build clean.** Locked by the `midpoint-creates-its-endpoints` and `diameter-in-a-circle-defined-by-centre-radius` scenarios + the extended `circle-on-diameter.test.ts`. Diagnosed from `logs/debug-log.jsonl`.

## ADR-092 — "AB קוטר במעגל O" / bare "AB קוטר": a GIVEN diameter defines the circle

**Status:** Accepted (2026-06-22, operator session).

**Context.** Continuing [ADR-090](#adr-090)/[ADR-091](#adr-091): after "B אמצע צלע AC" (so A,B exist), the operator typed **"AB קוטר במעגל O"** and it ERRORED **"'B' is already defined"** — the "במעגל" (in a circle) routed it to `diameter` (add-to-existing, which makes B an antipode), but circle O didn't exist and A,B were GIVEN points. Bare **"AB קוטר"** was not-handled. The earlier define-marker discriminator (of/with/centre/radius) didn't cover the plain "in circle O" / bare phrasing.

**Decision.** The real discriminator is **whether there's an existing circle to attach the diameter to**, not just the phrasing. `circleOnDiameter` now also fires for a **GIVEN diameter**: both endpoints already exist AND the referenced circle is missing (a named circle that doesn't exist, or no circle at all) — then "AB קוטר [במעגל O]" means "make a circle with diameter AB" (centre = midpoint AB). The circle keyword is no longer required in this given-diameter case (bare "AB קוטר" works). Three guards keep the add-path intact: a named-and-existing circle returns early (→ `diameter`); a cyclic "AD קוטר במעגל ABCD" (circle exists, ctx.circles non-empty) stays `diameter`; and "diameter DE in circle O" with NEW endpoints (endpointsExist=false) stays `diameter`.

**Result.** "AB קוטר במעגל O" and bare "AB קוטר" (with A,B placed) define a circle with AB as diameter (|OA|=|OB|, centre at the midpoint); the cyclic add-a-diameter and the new-points "diameter DE in circle O" paths are unchanged. **1237 tests green, build clean.** Locked by the `given-diameter-defines-circle` scenario + the extended `circle-on-diameter.test.ts` (given-diameter block). Diagnosed from `logs/debug-log.jsonl`. (Reinforces the recurring lesson: a STANDARD construct that escalates to the LLM and "builds nothing" is a parser gap to close, not the LLM's job.)

## ADR-093 — a circle's through-point counts as ON the circle (no tangent-at-the-through-point cycle)

**Status:** Accepted (2026-06-22, operator session).

**Context.** Figure: B = midpoint of AC; "AB קוטר" → circle O with diameter AB, built as `circle-through(O, A)` so **A is the through-point that defines O's radius**; a tangent from E at D; C on the extension of ED; then **"EA משיק למעגל בנקודה A"** (EA tangent at A). It ERRORED **"unresolved dependencies for: A, B, O, …, circle-O, …"** (then the LLM built nothing). The operator correctly noted this is **not a new construct** — it is the existing tangent-at-a-point ([ADR-081](#adr-081)).

**Root cause.** The tangent-at-an-endpoint path emits `point-on-circle A` + `set-perpendicular(O, A, E, A)`. `pointOnCircle` recognised on-circle/antipode/arc-midpoint/line-circle/circle-circle/circumcentre members but **not a `circle-through` point** — even though that point lies on the circle by construction. So the idempotent case (a) was missed, and the apply fell to case (c2), which **converted A into an on-circle point**. But A also *defines* the circle's radius (`radius.via='through', point=A`), so A now depended on circle-O and circle-O on A → a **dependency cycle** → "unresolved dependencies".

**Decision.** `pointOnCircle` now returns true when `id` is the circle's through-point (`radius.via === 'through' && radius.point === id`). A through-point IS on its circle, so "P on circle" for that point becomes the **idempotent no-op** (case a) instead of a re-definition — no cycle. Only the tangency constraint (OA ⟂ EA) is then added, flexing the figure to a real tangent at A.

**Result.** The operator's exact sequence builds; OA ⟂ EA holds (true tangent at A). General fix (any tangent/relation naming a circle's through-point), correct everywhere `pointOnCircle` is used (it's a true predicate now). **1239 tests green, build clean** (no regressions — case (c) grow-to-a-different-point and (c2) free-vertex-slide are unaffected; through-point is caught earlier). Locked by the `tangent-at-a-diameter-endpoint-no-cycle` scenario. Diagnosed from `logs/debug-log.jsonl`.

## ADR-094 — two tangents from an external point when a touch point already exists

**Status:** Accepted (2026-06-22, operator session).

**Context.** Figure: B=mid AC; "AB קוטר" → circle O with A a diameter endpoint already ON it; then **"מנקודה E יוצאים משיקים למעגל בנקודות A ו D"** (from E, two tangents touching at A and D) → ERRORED **"'A' is already defined"** → LLM built nothing. `tangentsFromExternal` builds the two touch points with a Thales **circle∩circle** (`circle-circle-intersection id:A`, `id:D`), which RE-CREATES A — conflicting with the existing A. The single-tangent case already deferred for an existing touch ([ADR-081](#adr-081)) and the through-point cycle was fixed ([ADR-093](#adr-093)), but the **two-tangent** rule had neither.

**Decision.** When EITHER named touch point already exists, `tangentsFromExternal` falls back to the tangency-**constraint** form (the two-tangent generalisation of ADR-081/093): for each touch P, emit `point-on-circle P` (idempotent if P is already on the circle — ADR-093 — or created with a free θ if new) + `set-perpendicular(O, P, E, P)` (EP ⟂ OP) + segment E–P. The figure then flexes so both EA and ED are real tangents from E, distinct (different seeds/solver branches), both on the circle. When BOTH touch points are new, the proven deterministic **Thales** construction is unchanged.

**Result.** "two tangents from E at A and D" (A an existing diameter endpoint, D new) builds: OA ⟂ AE and OD ⟂ DE (both tangent), A ≠ D, both on the circle. The all-new two-tangent path (FR-EN-18) is unchanged. **1240 tests green, build clean.** Locked by the `two-tangents-one-touch-already-exists` scenario.

**Process note (operator feedback).** Verified through a harness that mirrors **`App.submit`** exactly — `parseCtx` → the dropped-label gate → `dryRunOutcome` → `execute` through the store — not just the `parse → replay` path. A `parse → replay` test confirms the *engine* builds the commands, but the **app** can still refuse to commit (a `dryRunOutcome` error escalates to the LLM, which builds nothing → "doesn't build"). The app-path harness reproduces that exactly, so a fix is only called done when the operator's verbatim utterance **commits** through it.

## ADR-095 — a constraint on a secant crossing can drive the free DOFs upstream of it

**Status:** Accepted (2026-06-22, operator session).

**Context.** A tangent/secant figure — circle O, external B, tangent BC, secant B→A→E (A,E on the circle), chord CD ∥ EA, K = ED∩AC, |AK|=3, |ED|=7 — then **"∠CAE = 45"** → **"over-constrained: ∠CAE = 45° cannot hold"** (→ LLM, nothing). The operator's question: what's wrong with the angle. A scan proved it is **NOT** genuinely over-determined: the figure has free DOFs (the external point B, the free radius), and ∠CAE ranges ~2°–143° across them — **45° is reachable**.

**Root cause.** ∠CAE references C, A, E — all derived (C a tangent point, A a secant crossing) or already consumed (E). `driveOrCheck` finds no directly-referenced free carrier → pushes it as a check → over-constraint. The failure-path `recruitFreeDofs` should then drive the *upstream* free DOFs (B, radius) — but the ancestor walker `ancestors`/`pointParents` had **no case for `line-circle` (or `circle-circle`) points**: they hit `pointParents`'s `default → []` and the walk **dead-ended**, so `freeDrivableAncestors` returned `[]` and recruit found nothing (verified by instrumenting: `cand = []`).

**Decision.** In the `ancestors` walk's `drivable` mode, traverse a **secant crossing** (`line-circle`) through its **line** (→ the line's points → the external apex B). A `circle-circle` crossing is deliberately **not** traversed: its Thales-aux chain reaches too many DOFs and **over-recruits** (it broke the [ADR-093](#adr-093) collinear step in testing) — and the secant path already reaches the apex, so the angle is satisfiable without it.

**Result.** "∠CAE = 45" now builds: the angle comes out 45.00°, with |AK|=3, |ED|=7, CD∥EA all intact — the solver drives B (recruited via the secant) to the reachable 45° configuration. **1242 tests green, build clean** (the over-recruiting circle∩circle variant was caught by the ADR-093 regression and dropped). Locked by `recruit-through-secant.test.ts`.

**Process note.** This was validated the right way after the operator's "test it yourself" feedback: (1) a scan proved 45° reachable (so it's a solver gap, not a true over-constraint); (2) instrumenting `recruitFreeDofs` showed `cand=[]` (the walk dead-ended) — pinpointing the cause; (3) the first fix (also traversing circle∩circle) was REJECTED by the full suite (ADR-093 regression), and narrowed to secant-only. A green unit test plus the full suite — not a claim — is the bar.

## ADR-096 — the NOUN/definitional form of line∩circle ("E is the מפגש of AO with the circle")

**Status:** Accepted (2026-06-22, operator session).

**Context.** The operator asked whether the tool can parse **"נקודה E היא מפגש של AO עם המעגל"** ("point E is the meeting of AO with the circle"). It returned `not-handled` (→ LLM). Yet the **verb** forms of the same fact already worked: "AO חותך/פוגש את המעגל בנקודה E" and "AO meets the circle at E" all built the correct `line-circle-intersection`. So the capability existed; only this phrasing fell through.

**Root cause (two gaps in `lineMeetsCircle`).** (1) `INTERSECT_KW` carried the **verb** meeting forms (`נפגש`/`פוגש`/`פגש`, `\bmeets?\b`) but **not the noun** `מפגש` ("a meeting/junction") or English "meeting" — so the rule's gate never fired. (The line∩line noun handler `bisectorIntersection` already special-cased `מפגש`; the circle path never got the same treatment — note `חיתוך`/`intersection` *did* pass via the substring `intersect`.) (2) The new-point detector `crossingAfterCircle` only finds a label named **after** the circle ("…המעגל בנקודה E"); in the definitional form the point is declared **first** ("נקודה E היא …") with nothing after the circle, so it returned null.

**Decision.** A general "noun/definitional phrasing" fix for line∩circle, not a special case: (1) `lineMeetsCircle` accepts the noun keyword (`מפגש`/`meeting`) in addition to `INTERSECT_KW`; (2) a new helper `leadingNamedPoint` reads the result point named ahead of the construction (`[ה]נקודה E היא …` / `point E is …` / a leading `E is`/`E =`), and the rule strips that leading declaration before `labelRun` so the line's two endpoints still parse. This mirrors how line∩line already supports the noun form, keeping the two paths consistent.

**Result.** All seven phrasings (verb + noun, He + En) now build the identical `line-circle-intersection`. **1243 tests green, build clean.** Locked by the `point-is-meeting-of-line-with-circle` scenario.

## ADR-097 — convex-by-default is a SOLVER preference, not just a seeder rule: determined shapes draw convex when a convex solution exists

**Status:** Accepted (2026-06-22, operator session).

**Context.** Operator built `מרובע BCED חסום במעגל` → external `A = BD∩CE` (`המשך BD והמשך CE נפגשים בנקודה A`) → `AE=2CE` → `AD=CE`. Two complaints: (1) the quad BCED drew **crossed** (a bowtie — side BC crosses ED), and we'd agreed shapes should be convex in their letter order; (2) the DOF cue read **"5 DOF"** but "show another configuration" said **impossible**.

**Root cause.** The general inscribed quad placed its four vertices at the convex-default angles `CYCLIC_QUAD_ANGLES` with an **explicit `theta` and no `free` flag**, and `apply` derives `free = (theta === undefined)` — so the vertices were **pinned** (`free=false, solve=false`). That is an [ADR-052](#adr-052) violation (unstated magnitudes frozen as fixed). With B,C pinned, the two constraints could only drive E,D, and **no convex BCED satisfies the constraints with B,C fixed** — a convex solution exists only when all four vertices are free (confirmed numerically: ~half the solution family is convex). So the solve was boxed into a crossed branch, and the cue (`rawMovableDof − constraints = 7 − 2 = 5`) counted B,C,E,D as freedom that was in fact unsampleable (B,C pinned) or driven (E,D) → resample saw only the similarity DOF (placement + radius) → "impossible".

**Principle established.** *Convex-by-default is a preference over **valid** drawings, applied by the solver — not only the seeder.* A stated constraint always wins (draw what the givens determine), but among all constraint-satisfying drawings the solver **prefers a convex one**; it accepts a concave/crossed drawing only when **no** convex solution satisfies the givens. (Confirmed with the operator: "convex shapes are always the default as long as such a quad exists.") A genuinely-concave figure is therefore reached only when the givens force it (e.g. a stated reflex angle `∠BCD=220°`, a point placed inside) — never by accident.

**Decision (three coordinated changes).**
1. **General-quad vertices are FREE (the convex angles are a STARTING position).** `point-on-circle` gains an optional `free` flag; `apply` uses `free ?? (theta === undefined)`. The inscribed *general* quad emits its convex-default angles **with `free: true`** (seed 0 stays convex; shaped polygons — square/rect/rhombus/trapezoid — keep their intrinsic fixed angles).
2. **Convexity preference in the driven solvers ([evaluate.ts](../src/engine/evaluate.ts)).** `solutionAccepted` gains `requireConvex`; `resolveMixedCarriers`/`resolveFreeDriven` run a **convex-required pass first** and fall back to the relaxed accept only if no convex solution is reachable. A no-op when the figure declares no ≥4-gon.
3. **Recruit free polygon vertices on a convex-failing coupled solve.** When the convex pass can't satisfy the constraints with the directly-referenced carriers, `resolveMixedCarriers` recruits the declared polygon's other **free** on-circle vertices as extra DOF (no new constraint) and retries convex — giving a constrained quad enough freedom to reshape convex. Gated on convex-failure, so figures that already solve convex are untouched. The recruited vertices stay `free`, so "show another configuration" can vary them.

**Result.** The figure now draws **convex** (BCED in cyclic order), both constraints hold (AE=2·CE, AD=CE), the verifier is green, and `freeDofs` includes B,C → "show another configuration" offers 12/16 convex alternatives (was 0). **1245 tests green, build clean.** Locked by scenarios `constrained-inscribed-quad-stays-convex` (seed 0) and `constrained-inscribed-quad-resample` ("show another").

**Deferred (build when a corpus problem needs it).** The explicit concave OPT-IN — a stated reflex angle (`∠BCD=220°`, the precise mechanism) and/or the `קעור`/"concave" keyword (FR-EN-16 sugar, cycling which vertex is reflex). Not built now: no current corpus problem needs a non-determined concave quad.

---

## ADR-098 — a free point's placement is SAMPLED to satisfy a directional extension ("המשך"), never DRIVEN across the tangent degeneracy

**Status.** Accepted (2026-06-22).

**Context.** Operator (session `n19qmb3t`): two circles meet at A,B; `נקודה C על מעגל P` (C a **free** point on circle P); `המשך CA חותך מעגל O בנקודה D`; `המשך CB חותך מעגל O בנקודה E`. Report: *"point C is not positioned in a place that can satisfy the input."* Traced through the real pipeline: at the sampled C (top of circle P) C is **outside** circle O, so on line CB the *far* crossing of circle O **is B itself** — the only other crossing falls **between C and B**, so E landed on the near side and the directional `המשך` ("beyond B") was violated. The figure nonetheless showed **green (verified)**. For *both* extensions to reach circle O's far side, C must sit on a specific arc (each named endpoint the **near** crossing); a valid placement exists (≈8% of sampled seeds) but the default/sampled one wasn't it.

**Root cause (three converging gaps).**
