1. The `extend-onto-circle` **shared-endpoint branch** ([apply.ts](../src/engine/apply.ts)) deterministically picks "the other crossing, avoiding the shared endpoint" with **no record of the directional intent** (unlike the neither-on-circle branch, which emits `collinear-order`).
2. C's **free θ was sampled blind** to the order — `resample` accepted on convexity/distinctness/shape-differs only, never "does each extension reach the far side."
3. The **verifier re-derived neither** the on-circle membership nor the order for `extend-onto-circle`, and order constraints were treated as soft selectors — so the wrong-side figure was a silent green.

The existing `two-circles-mutual-tangent-secants` scenario was found to have the **same latent wrong-side bug** at seed 0 (its check asserted collinearity but never order).

**Decision (operator chose SAMPLE/GATE, never drive).** C is genuinely a free DOF, so its value is **sampled** to a satisfying placement — *not* solved. Adding `collinear-order` to the construction was rejected: `evaluate` hard-fails on every constraint, so it would trigger `recruitFreeDofs` to **drive** C across the E=B tangent degeneracy (a collapse the deterministic branch was built to avoid). Instead:
1. **Verifier** ([verify.ts](../src/engine/verify.ts)) re-derives `extend-onto-circle`'s on-circle membership AND a **hard directional-order check** (the new point must be beyond the second endpoint along a→b) — re-derived from the command stream, independent of how the point was built. A wrong-side figure now goes **amber**, not silent-green.
2. **Sampler gate** ([geoStore.ts](../src/store/geoStore.ts)) — `resample` ("show another configuration") and a new `firstSatisfyingSeed` accept a seed only if every extension reaches the far side by a **clean margin** (`extensionsClear`, beyond the endpoint by ≥5% of the figure span — rejects a near-tangent secant whose new point nearly collapses onto the endpoint). The sampling bar is stricter than the verifier's amber bar (a marginal-but-genuinely-beyond figure is valid, not "wrong").
3. **Auto-pick the default** — `execute` auto-advances the seed to the first satisfying configuration when a new step leaves the current view wrong; the scenario harness `run()` mirrors this (returns 0 for any figure without an extension, so non-extension scenarios are unchanged).

**Result.** The operator's exact sequence now auto-advances to a valid default (both D beyond A and E beyond B, verifier clean); the latent mutual-tangent bug is fixed and that scenario now asserts the order. Locked by `free-point-on-circle-both-extensions-reach-far-side` (replay path) + `free-on-circle-extensions-auto-advance` (store path) + order assertions on `two-circles-mutual-tangent-secants`. **1247 tests green, build clean.**

**Known broader gap (deferred).** A constraint that needs to drive a free point *across* a tangent degeneracy (where the two crossings merge) is still out of reach — here it's avoided by sampling because the figure is under-determined. A figure that is *determined* into such a configuration would need a different mechanism.

---

## ADR-099 — inscribing in an EXISTING named circle asserts membership, never re-creates the circle

**Status.** Accepted (2026-06-22).

**Context.** Operator (session `99j7krj3`, the full bagrut Q4): two circles meet at A,B; C on the right circle; `המשך CA`/`המשך CB` put D,E on the LEFT circle O; then `מרובע EBAD חסום במעגל O` ("quadrilateral EBAD inscribed in circle O") → **weak:error → LLM built-nothing** (twice). The four vertices E,B,A,D and circle O all **already exist** (A,B are O∩P; D,E are line∩O — so all four are already ON circle O).

**Root cause.** The `inscribedPolygon` rule, on `allExist`, always emitted `circumcircle(circle-O, E,B,A)` to build the circumscribing circle — but circle O **already exists**, so the command re-created it and **redefined its centre O** (`'O' is already defined — it can't be redefined as something different`), and the whole step was dropped. The rule discriminated on whether the *vertices* exist, never on whether the *named circle* exists.

**Decision.** When the inscribe phrase names a circle that **already exists**, the rule does **not** re-create it. It asserts membership per vertex — `point-on-circle id circle-O`, which is **idempotent** for a point already on the circle (an intersection / `line∩circle` / through-point, ADR-093) and **converts** a free vertex to slide on it — and draws the polygon. Works whether the vertices pre-exist or are fresh. The create-a-new-circle paths (a `circumcircle` through three existing points, or a fresh circle for new vertices) are unchanged for the circle-does-not-exist case.

**Result.** `מרובע EBAD חסום במעגל O` now builds: E,B,A,D asserted on the existing O (idempotent), the quad drawn, **no duplicate circle**. The book's part-א theorem holds in the resulting figure (∠EDA = ∠CBA, exactly, across configurations). Locked by scenario `inscribe-existing-points-in-existing-circle`. **1248 tests green, build clean.**

---

## ADR-100 — angle EQUALITY ("∠ABC = ∠DEF") is a parser rule over the existing angle-ratio constraint

**Status.** Accepted (2026-06-22).

**Context.** Operator (sessions `99j7krj3`/`f2gyj40u`, bagrut Q4): typing an **angle equality** — `∠GEC=∠CBA`, `∠GEC=∠CHA` (the proof's relations) — returned **not-understood**, then escalated to the LLM (also not-understood). The `angle` rule only matches a numeric value (`∠GAB = 37`), and `measureAngle` only a symbolic value (`∠ABC = 2α`); a *two-angle* equality matched neither and fell through.

**Root cause.** A **parser gap**, not an engine gap. The engine already represents angle equality — `set-angle-ratio` with `k = 1` (`∠1 = k·∠2`), the exact relation the `similarity` rule emits for AA. There was simply no rule for the bare "∠ … = ∠ …" phrasing.

**Decision.** Add an `angleEquality` rule (before `measureAngle`/`angle`): split on the single `=`, read a 3-letter angle (middle letter = vertex) on each side, and emit `set-angle-ratio {v1,a1,b1, v2,a2,b2, k}`. Supports Hebrew "זווית" and the `∠`/`∢` symbol, and an optional coefficient on the right (`∠ABC = 2∠DEF` → `k = 2`). Both arms of each angle are drawn (idempotent segments), mirroring the `angle` rule. It fires only when **both** sides are angles — a numeric RHS (`= 37`) still routes to `angle`, a symbolic RHS (`= 2α`) to `measureAngle`, and a bare segment equality (`AB = CD`, no angle marker) is untouched. On an under-determined figure the constraint drives a free DOF until the angles match; on a determined one it is a check (the givens verifier flags it if it cannot hold).

**Result.** `∠GEC=∠CBA` and the like now parse and apply. Exercised end-to-end with the book's own part-א theorem `∠EDA = ∠CBA` on the Q4 figure (parses, applies, holds for every configuration). Locked by `parser/__tests__/angle-equality.test.ts` (8 cases) + scenario `angle-equality-on-q4`. **1257 tests green, build clean.**

**Note (DOF cue).** The operator also questioned the "8 degrees of freedom" cue on the Q4 figure. Verified: 8 was correct under the *then* convention (raw − constraints, counting the placement+rotation+scale gauge — "a lone square has 4 DOF"). The genuine *shape* freedom (modulo similarity) is 8 − 4 = 4; the operator chose to switch the cue to report that shape DOF — see [ADR-101](#adr-101).

---

## ADR-101 — the "degrees of freedom" cue reports SHAPE DOF (modulo similarity), so a determined shape reads 0

**Status.** Accepted (2026-06-22). Refines [ADR-018](#adr-018) Stage 3.

**Context.** Operator, on the bagrut-Q4 figure: *"check dof — it says 8 but I'm not sure this is the case."* The cue counted **raw movable DOF − constraints** (ADR-018 Stage 3), which includes the full **placement + rotation + scale** freedom: the Q4 figure's 8 = O(2) + P(2) + radius-O(1) + radius-P(1) + C(1) + F(1). Two problems: (1) the number is **inflated** by the 4-dimensional similarity gauge, so it doesn't match a student's sense of "how much about the *shape* is still free" (genuine shape DOF = 8 − 4 = 4); (2) the **"✓ fully determined"** emphasis (FR-ALT-4, fires at count = 0) was **unreachable** for any normal figure — even a rigid SSS triangle showed 3 (its placement gauge), and the badge only appeared if points were pinned to absolute coordinates (which the bagrut workflow never does).

**Decision (operator chose "switch to shape-DOF").** `freeDofCount` now subtracts the FREE part of the similarity gauge: **shape DOF = raw − constraints − gauge**, where `gauge = translation(2, gone once any point is pinned) + rotation(1, present with ≥2 points, gone with ≥2 pins) + scale(1, present until a numeric distance / fixed-length radius / ≥2 pins fixes it)`. Length constraints that pin scale are ALREADY in the constraint count, so the gauge omits scale then (no double-count). Result: a figure rigid **up to similarity** reads **0** → "✓ fully determined" fires when the SHAPE is pinned down, not only when coordinates are. The samplable-DOF *list* (`freeDofs`) is unchanged, so "show another configuration" still varies place/size (a determined shape can still be shown at other sizes/orientations).

**Worked values.** lone square → 0 (was 4); square + AB=5 → 0; triangle → 2 (its two angles; was 6); SSS triangle → 0 (was 3); single circle → 0; parallelogram → 2 (was 6); parallelogram + |AB|=|AC| → 1; segment+segment+⟂ → 3 (was 7); **bagrut Q4 → 4** (was 8). Locked by the updated `phase-sample.test.ts`.

**Trade-off / note.** "✓ fully determined" can now coexist with an active "show another configuration" button (the shape is fixed, but its similarity image — size/orientation/position — can still vary, which is meaningful when no size was stated, ADR-052). This is intended: "determined" describes the *shape*, not the page placement.

---

## ADR-103 — a constraint can drive a circle's CENTRE, not only its radius

**Status.** Accepted (2026-06-23). Closes (in part) the ADR-095 "known broader gap".

**Context.** Operator (session `zqvtvh15`, bagrut Q4): two circles meet at A,B; C on circle P; D = CA extended onto circle O; then a size given — `CD = 36` (and ultimately `CE⟂AB` with `CD=36, DE=18`, part ד) — failed **"over-constrained: |CD| = 36 cannot hold"**. The figure is small by default (|CD| ≈ 8).

**Root cause.** `recruitFreeDofs` (via `ancestors` in `drivable` mode) surfaced a circle's free **radius** as a DOF ([ADR-051](#adr-051)) but **never its free centre**, and `ancestors` does **not** traverse a circle∩circle point (the [ADR-095](#adr-095) over-recruit guard). So the circle **centres** O, P — free points — were unreachable from a constraint on A/B/C/D (points that ride the circles). With the centres pinned a fixed gap apart, the **circle∩circle geometry caps |CD|** (~8 here) no matter how large the radii grow — so |CD| = 36 was genuinely unsolvable for the engine, even though a real configuration exists (the centres just need to spread; with centres free, |CD| reaches 40+).

**Decision.** In `ancestors` (drivable mode), where a point's circle's free **radius** is surfaced as a DOF, also surface the circle's **centre** when it is a free, non-pinned point. A constraint on a point that lies on a circle can then drive the centre as well as the radius. This is the **targeted** form of the ADR-095 gap (surface the specific centre DOF rather than blanket-traversing a circle∩circle's Thales-aux chain), so it doesn't over-recruit: the joint solver's regulariser keeps a surfaced-but-unneeded centre near its seed, and **the full suite stayed green** (the ADR-095 regression the blanket traversal caused did not recur).

**Result.** `CD = 36` now solves (the centres spread; |CD| = 36, verifier green), and the **full bagrut-Q4 given set** — `∠GEC=∠CHA`, `CD=36`, `DE=18`, `CE⟂AB` — builds when the size givens precede the ⟂. Locked by `engine/__tests__/recruit-circle-center.test.ts` (the |CD|=36 unit + the full-Q4 build) and scenario `distance-drives-circle-centres-apart`. **1260 tests green, build clean.**

**Known remaining limitation (tracked, not fixed here).** Entering `CE⟂AB` **before** the size givens (`CD=36, DE=18`) — an under-determined coupled solve over C's angle + both circle centres + radii + the extension point G, coupled through the G→F→H derived chain — still does **not** converge from the default seed (a feasible configuration exists; the local/coordinate-descent/binding multi-start can't reach it). Adding the sizes first makes it solve. A coordinate-descent angular sweep over the on-circle DOF and an extension-carrier grid-scan range fix were tried and did **not** crack this case, so they were not shipped. Robustly solving the bare under-determined coupled system would need a stronger global solver (deferred).

---

## ADR-104 — order-independence: a constraint that can't be satisfied yet is DEFERRED and retried after later givens

**Status.** Accepted (2026-06-23).

**Context.** Operator (bagrut Q4): "the diagram should build the same no matter what order the data was entered — if the engine can't solve it one way, it should try differently." Concretely, `CE⟂AB` typed **before** the size givens `CD=36, DE=18` failed "over-constrained", yet the same figure builds when the sizes come first ([ADR-103](#adr-103)): without the sizes the figure is an **under-determined coupled solve** the numerical solver can't land (many DOFs free, no unique target); **with** them it is determinate and the ⟂ solves. So the result depended on entry order — fragile.

**Why not a stronger solver.** The bare under-determined case (CE⟂AB + ∠GEC=∠CHA, no sizes) was attacked directly — a heavy deterministic (Halton) global multi-start over the full 8-DOF space (C's angle + both circle centres + radii + the extension point) — and it **did not** converge: the feasible set is a thin manifold in 8-D and quasi-random sampling can't reliably hit its basin (curse of dimensionality). A general always-converging geometric solver is out of scope. The leverage is elsewhere: **adding the remaining givens makes the system determinate**, and the solver handles determinate systems well.

**Decision.** Make `replay` order-independent by **deferral**. After applying the facts in order (each kept-prior on failure), RETRY the still-failed **constraint-only** facts (those that introduce no new points) against the now-complete figure, to a fixpoint. Applying such a constraint *last* is exactly the working re-ordering — so a constraint typed too early is automatically re-tried after the givens that pin it. A genuinely contradictory constraint keeps failing (its error is still reported); a point-introducing fact is never deferred (re-ordering it would strand its dependents). This is "the engine tries differently" — engine-side, deterministic, no LLM round-trip.

**Result.** The full Q4 given set now builds **in every order** tested (`angle,perp,CD,DE` / `angle,CD,DE,perp` / `perp,angle,CD,DE` / `CD,DE,angle,perp` / `perp,CD,DE,angle` — all OK), and the operator's exact flow (CE⟂AB before the sizes) builds end-to-end. Figures with no failed facts are untouched (the retry loop is gated on `facts.some(deferrable)`); intentional over-constraints still fail honestly (the suite's over-constraint tests stay red-on-purpose). Locked by scenario `q4-constraints-order-independent`. **1261 tests green, build clean.**

**Scope / limits.** Deferral gives order-independence for a COMPLETE, consistent given set — it re-orders failed constraints to the end. It does NOT make a genuinely under-determined partial figure (e.g. CE⟂AB with the sizes never entered) snap to a configuration — that stays pending until the determining givens arrive (correct: there's no unique figure yet). The "decision of what point is fixed" the operator objected to is gone for these cases: every unstated magnitude (G's extension distance, C's angle, the circle centres/radii) is a free DOF the solver may drive, and a derived point (F from G) re-evaluates from its parents — nothing is artificially pinned.

**Amendment (2026-06-23) — the INPUT GATE must commit a deferrable constraint, not escalate it.** The first cut put deferral in `replay`, which operates on the COMMITTED fact list — but the operator re-ran the flow and it still failed on the ⟂. Root cause: `App.submit` dry-runs a parsed step (`dryRunOutcome`) before committing, and a constraint that can't be satisfied at its position returns `reason: 'error'`, which the gate treated as "weak" → **escalated to the LLM** (which re-emits the same command, or drops it) → the ⟂ **never entered the fact list**, so `replay`'s deferral never saw it. Fix: when a deterministic parse (no dropped labels) yields a step whose dry-run errors but which carries a **deferrable constraint** (`hasDeferrableConstraint` — a `set-perpendicular`/`set-distance`/`set-angle`/… relation that introduces no new point), `App.submit` now **commits it** instead of escalating. It shows as a failing step until later givens pin it, at which point deferral resolves it; a genuine contradiction surfaces honestly as a failing step rather than a misleading "couldn't read that". Locked by the App.submit-faithful test `q4-commit-deferred-perpendicular` (the operator's exact sequence through parse → gate → execute). **1262 tests green.**

---

## ADR-105 — a DRIVEN extension point stays on its extension (t>1); "show another configuration" shows a working state

**Status.** Accepted (2026-06-23).

**Context.** Operator (session `3yvigwa7`): triangle ABC inscribed; D = arc-midpoint of BC; E on the **extension** of chord DC; then `∠CAE = 50`. E ended up **between D and C** (param ≈ 0.16), not on the extension — *"point E didn't respect that it needed to be after D"*. Separately: pressing **"show another configuration"** gave **no feedback** while the (synchronous) search ran — *"no sign of the system thinking; the user needs to know to wait."*

**Root cause (the order).** `∠CAE = 50` **drives** E (recruited as a carrier). The driven solver treated E's on-segment parameter as living in **[0,1]** (the segment interior): the single-carrier root finder bracketed `[0,1]`, the mixed-carrier grid/binding seed used `[0,1]`, and the unbounded joint Nelder–Mead then pulled E to t≈0.16 to satisfy the angle. Nothing kept E on the **extension** (t>1), even though `extension: true` says it lives beyond the segment by definition.

**Decision (order).** An **extension** on-segment carrier is solved with t>1, three coordinated changes in [evaluate.ts](../src/engine/evaluate.ts): (1) the single-carrier `range()` returns `[1.02, 12]` for an extension (so `drivenRoots` only finds beyond-segment roots); (2) the mixed-carrier range uses `[1.02, 12]`; (3) — the one that actually binds the unbounded optimiser — `setCarrierVals` **hard-clamps** a driven extension point to `t ≥ 1.02`, so the joint solver can't place E between the endpoints and must instead move the figure's OTHER free DOFs (here the triangle) to satisfy the angle. Result: E lands at t≈3.24 (beyond C) with `∠CAE = 50` and `∠ABC = 60` both holding, no violations, on every seed. Locked by scenario `driven-extension-point-stays-beyond`.

**Decision (feedback).** "Show another configuration" runs a synchronous multi-seed search that can take a moment on a heavy figure and froze the UI silently. The button now paints a **busy state first** (double `requestAnimationFrame` so the "thinking…" label + disabled button render before the blocking search) then runs the search. (Engine-side; the search itself stays synchronous and deterministic.)

**Result.** **1264 tests green, build clean.** The extension-clamp is general — any constraint-driven extension point now stays on its extension (the directional given), not just this figure.

---

## ADR-106 — verify-then-search: before drawing, auto-search seeds + branches for a configuration that meets every requirement

**Status.** Accepted (2026-06-23).

**Context.** Operator: *"do we have a mechanism that, before drawing the solution, checks it meets all requirements and if not loops on other options?"* We had the **check** — the givens verifier ([verify.ts](../src/engine/verify.ts), [ADR-053](#adr-053)) re-derives every stated relation and reports `violations` — but it only **reported** (amber). Auto-looping existed only in pieces: the per-step multi-start (`solutionAccepted`), `firstSatisfyingSeed` (extension orders only, [ADR-098](#adr-098)), deferral ([ADR-104](#adr-104)). Nothing ran the **full** verifier and then searched alternative configurations for a clean one. The operator chose to build it over **seeds AND discrete branches**.

**Decision.** A general pre-draw resolver in [geoStore.ts](../src/store/geoStore.ts):
- `meetsRequirements(facts, seed)` — the unified bar: builds, **verifier-clean** (`violations` empty), every extension reaches its far side, points distinct, declared polygons convex. (A genuinely under-determined **pending** figure passes — its unsatisfied constraint is not a violation, [ADR-104]; there's nothing to search for.)
- `findValidConfig(facts, fromSeed)` — if the current config doesn't meet the bar, loop: first the **current branches** over a wide seed sweep (continuous DOFs), then **alternative discrete branch combinations** (which intersection / arc side / root — bounded to the first few branchable points and capped combinations) each over a short seed sweep. Returns the chosen facts (branches set) + seed, or null. Deterministic.
- `autoResolve()` store action — applies the found config (sets the branch facts + seed), or keeps the current figure (amber) if none found in budget.
- Wired into `App.submit`: after each step commits, `resolveAfterCommit()` runs `autoResolve` **behind a "thinking" state** (the search is synchronous and can be slow on a heavy figure; double-`requestAnimationFrame` paints the busy state first). It fires **only** when the freshly-built figure falls short — a clean or pending figure pays nothing.

**Result.** A figure that builds but doesn't satisfy a given (e.g. a wrong branch where the verifier flags a distance/angle) now auto-corrects to a verifier-clean drawing when one exists among the seeds/branches, before the student sees it; otherwise it stays amber (honest). Locked by `store/__tests__/auto-resolve.test.ts` (branch-flip + store action + clean-figure no-op). **1267 tests green, build clean.**

**Scope / limits.** The search is bounded (≈40 current-branch seeds + ≤16 branch combos × 6 seeds) — it is not an exhaustive solver; a deeply under-determined coupled figure can still need the operator to add givens (it then shows pending/amber). It prefers the current branch assignment and only changes branches when the current can't be made clean.

---

## ADR-107 — "XY bisects ∠… " onto an EXISTING point is a constraint, not a re-created intersection

**Status.** Accepted (2026-06-23).

**Context.** Operator (session `86cympns`): `G על DF` placed G on segment DF; then `EG חוצה זוית DEF` ("EG bisects ∠DEF") → **weak:error → built-nothing**. The angle-bisector treatment exists (`bisectorPlacesPoint`), but its "the segment's first letter is the angle vertex" branch always **created** the bisector-foot point as a `line∩line` (bisector ∩ the opposite side) — re-creating the already-placed G → **"'G' is already defined"**.

**Decision.** In that branch, if the foot point **already exists** (in `ctx.points`), don't re-create it — emit the bisector **constraint** instead: `∠(o1, vertex, D) = ∠(D, vertex, o2)` (a `set-angle-ratio`, k=1, i.e. ∠DEG = ∠GEF), which **drives the existing point** (here G, a free DOF on segment DF) onto the bisector. A genuinely new foot point is still constructed as before. (The rule gains `ctx`.)

**Result.** `EG חוצה זוית DEF` with a pre-existing G now builds: G is driven so EG bisects ∠DEF (∠DEG = ∠GEF), G staying on DF. Locked by scenario `bisector-onto-existing-point`. **1267 tests green, build clean.** (Same family as [ADR-086](#adr-086): a relation onto an EXISTING point is a constraint that drives it, not a redefinition.)

**Amendment (2026-06-23) — "A אמצע CD" onto an existing on-segment point.** Operator (session `lqtx8fn5`): A was placed on side CD, then `A אמצע CD` (A is the midpoint of CD) → "'A' is already defined" → built-nothing. The midpoint redefinition reached `reinterpretAsConstraint`, but `freeCarrierAncestor` searched only A's ANCESTORS (C,D — free vertices, not 1-DOF param carriers), never A itself, so it found no carrier and gave up. Fix: for a `midpoint` redefinition, use P's OWN free on-segment/on-circle DOF as the carrier — drive A's `t` to the midpoint of CD (the operator's working `AD=AC` was the manual equivalent). Scoped to `midpoint` so the point-on-segment (`reinterpretAsCollinear`) and second-placement-pins-an-ancestor (ADR-028) reinterpretations keep their behaviour. Locked by scenario `midpoint-of-existing-on-segment-point`. **1268 tests green.**

**Amendment 2 (2026-06-23) — generalised to the WHOLE placement family ("when a point exists and is referenced, use it").** Operator: "what other points/segments have this issue? fix all of it." Audit: a redefinition of an existing point reaches `reinterpretAsConstraint`, whose carrier was only a free *ancestor* — so any point whose OWN free DOF is the natural carrier (a point ON a segment between fixed vertices, an on-circle point, a free vertex) failed for EVERY placement type, not just midpoint. Generalised: (1) `reinterpretAsConstraint` defers `point-on-segment` to `reinterpretAsCollinear` and otherwise drives P's OWN free DOF — a non-extension on-segment `t`, an on-circle `θ`, or a non-pinned free point (x,y) — falling back to a free ancestor for a DERIVED or EXTENSION point (whose own clamp/0-DOF can't reach the target); (2) `driveCoincideOn` marks a free-point carrier too. Now midpoint / foot / arc-midpoint / circle∩circle / line∩line / a free-vertex redefinition all just CONSTRAIN the existing point. A PINNED point (explicit coords) still conflicts (it genuinely can't move); segments are idempotent (same `seg-AB` id dedupes) so they never had the issue. Locked by `engine/__tests__/redefine-existing-point.test.ts` (5 placement types). **1274 tests green, build clean.**

---

## ADR-108 — angle ACUTENESS: support "זווית קהה" (obtuse) and "זווית חדה" (acute)

**Status.** Accepted (2026-06-23).

**Context.** Operator: *"we need to support זווית קהה and זווית חדה"* (obtuse / acute angle). `∠C קהה` returned not-understood — there was no way to state that an angle must be obtuse (> 90°) or acute (< 90°), a common bagrut given/figure cue.

**Decision.** A new one-sided angle constraint `angle-acuteness {vertex, ray1, ray2, obtuse}`, modelled exactly like the [ADR-039](#adr-039) ordering inequalities: it is satisfied by a whole region (one side of 90°), has no sign change (optimizer-driven), removes **0 DOF**, and **actively reshapes** the figure — `recruitFreeDofs` drives a free DOF until the angle is on the requested side. One-sided residual: obtuse `max(0, 90 + MARGIN − ∠)`, acute `max(0, ∠ − (90 − MARGIN))`; tolerance accepts an angle at least `MIN_GAP°` past 90 (clearly obtuse/acute). Wired through every constraint switch (`solve.ts`), the joint-order minimisation and single-carrier order preference (`evaluate.ts`), the DOF count (`sample.ts` → 0), and `apply.ts` (`set-angle-acuteness`). Parser: a `angleAcuteness` rule reads `קהה`/`obtuse` and `חדה`/`acute` on a **three-letter** angle (`∠ABC`) OR a **single vertex** (`∠C`), whose two arms are resolved from the figure via a new `ParseContext.neighbors` (built by `pointNeighbors` — a point's segment/polygon-edge neighbours). The arms are drawn (idempotent), like `set-angle`.

**Result.** `∠C קהה` reshapes a triangle so ∠ACB > 90° (≈101°); `∠C חדה` → < 90° (≈72°); the three-letter form and English "obtuse/acute" also parse. Locked by `parser/__tests__/angle-acuteness.test.ts` (7), scenario `obtuse-acute-angle`, and a catalog entry (commands panel). **1282 tests green, build clean.**

## ADR-109 — Coordinate-validation campaign: a differential check against an independent closed-form oracle

**Status.** Accepted (2026-06-24) · built · 1286 tests green, build clean. The long-parked "differential coordinate-validation campaign" (see [09-implementation-plan.md](09-implementation-plan.md) session log, 2026-06-11). Complements — does not replace — the **invariants** campaign ([ADR-047](#adr-047) Tier-A; `engine/__tests__/campaign.test.ts`) and the **givens verifier** ([ADR-053](#adr-053)).

**Context — what the existing nets can and can't catch.** Two correctness nets already exist: the *invariants* campaign checks each generated figure against geometric RELATIONS a valid drawing must satisfy (true anywhere — `|MA|=|MB|`, perpendicularity, …); the *givens verifier* checks, live, that the relations a figure's COMMANDS asserted hold at the final coordinates ("green = verified"). Neither pins down an *exact position* against an *independent* source: a derived point can satisfy every checked relation and still be computed wrong, and the verifier only re-checks what was stated. A differential coordinate check closes that gap — but only where coordinates are unique.

**The DOF constraint (decided with the operator).** A figure with residual shape freedom (≥1 shape DOF — most real student figures) has no unique coordinate to diff against, so this method **only applies to figures determined up to similarity (0 shape DOF)**. We confirmed against the operator's own debug log that ~11% of successful figures are 0-shape-DOF (the rest are covered by the invariants + stability + verifier nets). So the campaign is a **sharp tool for the determined regime, generated synthetically** — it does not feed off the live log (under-determined, no ground truth) and is not a broad net.

**Decision — pinned-base + independent closed-form oracle, direct coordinate diff.** A deterministic seeded generator (`src/validation/`) emits recipes that **pin their base points** at known coordinates with `free-point` (so the figure has no free similarity gauge — the engine's frame equals the oracle's frame), then derive the rest with a real construct. An **oracle that imports nothing from the engine** (`coordOracle.ts`) recomputes the derived points' true coordinates by hand. Because the frame is pinned, the engine must reproduce them exactly — **no Procrustes alignment needed**. A branchy construct (circle∩circle, by-distances, a square's chirality) has several valid configurations; the engine passes iff its output matches one WHOLE configuration within tolerance (`compareToConfigs` — min over configs of the worst per-id distance). This is a genuine *differential* test: two independent implementations of the same spec must agree.

**Scope (v1).** Only constructs whose coordinates are independently computable in closed form AND fully determined by the pinned bases: midpoint, foot, line∩line, on-segment(t), by-distances, circle∩circle, distance/equal-driven on-segment (the numeric solver), square, parallelogram. **Out of scope:** shapes with unstated default DOFs (rectangle/rhombus/trapezoid height/angle) — their coordinates depend on engine defaults, not an independent spec, so they belong to the invariants campaign; and on-circle/arc/diameter points whose position rides an engine angle convention (a later slice can anchor them via intersections).

**Why this is additive and zero-risk.** The harness is a **read-only consumer of existing public engine exports** (`build`, `evaluate`) — like a test. It is not in the production bundle, modifies no engine/renderer/store/parser/app code, and has no side effects. Building and running it cannot break anything; only acting on a bug it *finds* touches core code (guarded by the suite, per CLAUDE.md rule 4). The independent oracle and the generator are precisely what cannot live in production (no ground truth for arbitrary under-determined student input), so this is a dev/CI tool by construction.

**Result.** `src/validation/coordOracle.ts` (pure geometry) + `coordCampaign.ts` (recipes, seeded generator, `compareToConfigs`, `runCoordCampaign` → structured report) + `__tests__/coordValidation.test.ts`. The test file proves three things: (1) a correct figure matches to machine precision; (2) **the harness CATCHES a wrong coordinate** — a perturbed/missing engine output is flagged (the load-bearing test — a differential checker that can't detect a discrepancy is worthless); (3) the committed corpus (172 figures) is a regression GATE asserting zero coordinate mismatches. `COORD_MULT` widens the seeded sweep for bug-hunting. **Finding: the engine matches the oracle to ~machine epsilon across the corpus — worst residual 5.6e-15 over a 13,760-figure (`COORD_MULT=80`) sweep**, vs a 1e-4 tolerance — i.e. the engine's coordinate output (including the numeric solver paths) is exact to double precision on every covered construct. **1286 tests green, build clean.** Next slices (deferred): a globally-solved/Procrustes-aligned mode (no pinned bases — pure solver test, e.g. SSS/AAS triangles), set-angle-driven points, and circle points anchored via intersections.

## ADR-110 — Named shapes from the theorem vocabulary, as parser-macro decompositions

**Status.** Accepted (2026-06-24). Prompted by an operator audit ("is there no support for דלתון?") that widened to *"are there other shapes/terms in the theorem list not covered?"* and the directive *"I want to have them"* under the standing thesis — **the student writes a figure *as it is*; the parser/LLM works out how.**

**Context.** Cross-referencing the bagrut theorem list ([07-theorem-reference.md](07-theorem-reference.md)) against the parser/engine vocabulary surfaced shapes a student could name but the app couldn't draw: **kite·דלתון** (th. 37–38, no construct), **isosceles triangle** (22–27), **equilateral triangle** (13), **isosceles trapezoid** (39–42), **triangle midsegment·קטע אמצעים** (62–67) — each buildable today only by hand-assembling constraints, with no named shape. (Circumcircle/incircle/median/altitude/⊥-bisector turned out already covered.) The operator's one rule: **the user must never get an error** — the LLM net may cover a missing construct, but the LLM is restricted to the catalog vocabulary and only avoids errors once the production proxy is deployed, so common shapes want a *deterministic* path.

**Decision.** Add each named shape as a **parser MACRO** (`shapeMacro` in `parse.ts`) — a keyword (He/En) + label run that emits a sequence of **already-supported canonical commands**, so the constraint solver does the work and **no new engine construct is needed**:

- **kite·דלתון** (also עפיפון) → `quadrilateral` + `|AB|=|AD|` + `|CB|=|CD|` (the free quad flexes into a kite; axis AC).
- **isosceles·שווה שוקיים** → `triangle` + `|AB|=|AC|` (apex = first vertex).
- **equilateral·שווה צלעות** → `triangle` + the three sides equal.
- **isosceles trapezoid·טרפז שווה שוקיים** → `trapezoid` (AB∥DC) + equal legs `|AD|=|BC|`.
- **midsegment·קטע אמצעים** → two `midpoint`s of the apex's two sides + the connecting `segment`.

The decomposition is sound and over-constraint-free (a general quad has 4 free vertices that two equal-side constraints drive jointly via `resolveFreeDriven` — there was already a passing sweep test `['triangle ABC','AB=AC']`). Theorem detection (Phase 6) re-derives the shape TYPE from geometry, so no engine `type` tag is needed even later. Each macro keeps the `quadShape`/`triShape` SHAPE_LEFTOVER discipline — a compound (`"kite ABCD with AB=6"`) **escalates** rather than half-parsing. The triangle macros also inherit `triShape`'s circumcircle-deferral guard (a triangle named *through/around* a circle — `חוסם`/`circumscribes` — is left to the circumcircle rules; regression `symbolic-2alpha-drives-shape-not-the-fixed-point`). Catalog entries (which auto-feed the LLM prompt), He/En coverage pairs, LLM decomposition examples, and the i18n `kite` term corrected `עפיפון`→`דלתון` (the curriculum term).

**Result.** All five parse in both locales and build a geometrically-correct figure (locked in `engine/__tests__/named-shapes.test.ts` + scenario `kite-named-shape`). Decompositions reuse the most-tested machinery; the LLM net covers the long tail (e.g. an irregular n>4 polygon → a segment loop). **1322 tests green, build clean.**

## ADR-111 — Regular polygon: a generic `polygon` command + on-circle equal spacing

**Status.** Accepted (2026-06-24). The one genuine engine gap from the ADR-110 audit (decided with the operator: build it deterministically; irregular n>4 stays on the LLM segment-loop net).

**Context.** The polygon family capped at 4 vertices — no construct could draw a regular pentagon/hexagon (th. 36, 89–90 reference n-gons). But the underlying machinery was **already n-ary**: the `polygon` object (`vertices: Id[]`), the renderer, and the convexity guard all loop over any n; only the *command* layer stopped at `triangle`/`quadrilateral`, and the `inscribedPolygon` rule already places vertices on a circle at chosen angles.

**Decision.** (1) A generic `{ type: 'polygon'; ids: Id[] }` command + a `polyEdges` helper (generalises `quadEdges`/`triEdges`: n boundary segments + the `poly-<ids>` object) + a minimal `case 'polygon'` in `apply.ts` (the vertices are placed by prior commands, so it only wires edges). (2) A `regularPolygon` parser rule: a regular n-gon = n vertices **equally spaced (360/n) on a HIDDEN circle** whose radius is a free, samplable DOF (ADR-052), reusing the inscribed-circle path. The corners are **pinned** (`point-on-circle` with `theta` set, `free` unset) because a regular polygon is rigid up to similarity. Only fires when regularity is explicit (`regular`/`משוכלל`/an `N-gon` form) — a bare "pentagon" (possibly irregular) is left to the LLM net, honouring ADR-052. Routing: n=3 → the equilateral macro, n=4 → `square`, n≥5 → the generic path. He names `מחומש/משושה/משובע/משומן`, En `pentagon…dodecagon`.

**Result.** A regular pentagon has 5 equal sides + 5 interior 108° angles; a hexagon 120°; "show another configuration" varies only size/placement (corners stay rigid). Locked in `named-shapes.test.ts` + scenario `regular-hexagon`. **1322 tests green.**

## ADR-112 — Shape-DOF cue: a pinned parametric vertex contributes 0 DOF (amends ADR-101)

**Status.** Accepted (2026-06-24). Refines [ADR-101](#adr-101) (`freeDofCount` reports SHAPE DOF modulo the similarity gauge). Decided with the operator while adding the regular polygon — surfaced as the one non-additive piece, and confirmed a pre-existing correctness gap, so fixed here uniformly.

**Context.** `rawMovableDof` counted **every** on-circle/on-segment point as 1 DOF, even when its θ/t is pinned. So a figure rigid up to similarity over-reported: an inscribed *square* read ~3 and a regular polygon ~4 instead of 0, and "✓ fully determined" never fired for them. This was already wrong for inscribed shapes before this work — the regular polygon just made it visible.

**Decision.** A parametric carrier (on-circle/on-segment) whose parameter is **explicit, non-free, non-driven** is geometrically rigid (like a pinned free point) → it contributes **0** raw DOF (`isPinnedParam` in `sample.ts`). A *free/samplable* parametric point still counts 1 (genuinely variable); a *driven* one still counts 1, balanced by the constraint that drives it (no double-count). This is the single correct rule that yields the right ladder — **free quad 4 → cyclic quad 3 → inscribed square 0 → regular polygon 0** — and is the natural sibling of the existing pinned-free-point special case.

**Result.** The ladder reads exactly (locked in `named-shapes.test.ts`); every inscribed/regular figure rigid up to similarity now reads 0 and fires "✓ fully determined". `freeDofs` (the samplable list) is untouched, so "show another configuration" still varies a determined shape's size/placement. The blast radius (all inscribed shapes) was re-verified against the full suite. **1322 tests green, build clean.**

## ADR-113 — The drivable-ancestor walk reaches the shape DOFs *behind* a parametric point

**Status.** Accepted (2026-06-24). Extends the constraint-recruitment family ([ADR-028](#adr-028) drive-or-recruit, [ADR-032](#adr-032) walk through constructed points, [ADR-051](#adr-051)/[ADR-103](#adr-103) reach a circle's free radius/centre). Operator-reported from a manual rhombus session (`logs/debug-log.jsonl` session `oew743rq`).

**Context.** Building a rhombus `ABCD`, then `E` = midpoint of `AB`, `G` on the **extension** of diagonal `BD` (beyond `D`), then `GE ⊥ AB`, failed with *"over-constrained: GE ⟂ AB cannot hold."* The figure is genuinely constructible: with `AB` horizontal and `E` at its midpoint, `GE ⊥ AB` forces `G` directly above `E`; on line `BD` that crossing sits beyond `D` only when the rhombus angle at `A` is **< 60°** (at exactly 60° the unique solution is the degenerate `G = D`). So satisfying it requires **flexing the rhombus angle** jointly with `G`'s extension parameter — a 1-parameter family of valid figures, not an over-constraint. A `git bisect` proved this **never worked** (back to 2026-06-17 the same figure failed with the more honest *"D and G would be at the same point"*): the engine only ever drove `G`, sliding it onto `D`.

**Root cause.** `ancestors(…, 'drivable')` — the recruiter's reachable-free-DOF walker used by `recruitFreeDofs` on the failure path — **stopped at a free 1-DOF parametric carrier** and never recursed past it. `G` is a free on-segment/extension carrier on `BD`; the rhombus's free shape DOF is its angle, carried by the `rotated` object `D` — which is `G`'s *parent*. Because the walk halted at `G`, `D` was unreachable, so the recruit surfaced only `G` (+ the placement gauge `A`,`B`, which can't change the shape) and the joint solver could only push `G` onto `D`.

**Decision.** In `drivable` mode, a free on-segment carrier is **recorded AND walked past** — push the carrier into the result, then continue to its `pointParents` to surface the shape DOFs behind its segment. (`param` mode still stops at a free carrier — only the joint-solve superset changed.) Effectively scoped to on-segment/extension points: `pointParents` of an on-circle carrier is `[]` (its circle DOFs are already surfaced separately), and a free vertex has no parents. Safe because (a) only the failure path (`recruitFreeDofs`) uses `drivable`, so eagerly-satisfiable figures never reach it, and (b) the joint solver's regulariser keeps a surfaced-but-unneeded parent at its seed.

**Result.** `GE ⊥ AB` now holds exactly (cos = 0) with `G` strictly beyond `D` (param `t ≈ 1.27`, `|GD| ≈ 1.2`) on every seed — the rhombus angle flexed below 60°. This is a general fix for the whole class (any constraint that must flex an upstream shape DOF reached *through* a parametric point), not a special-case. Locked by scenario `perpendicular-from-midpoint-flexes-rhombus`. **Full suite re-run for the blast radius — 1323 tests green, build clean.**

## ADR-114 — "Isosceles" picks a SOFT default equal-pair that yields to an explicit one

**Status.** Accepted (2026-06-24). Applies [ADR-052](#adr-052) (no fixed assumptions) to the named-shape macros of [ADR-110](#adr-110). Operator-reported from a manual session (`logs/debug-log.jsonl` session `yi4p8150`).

**Context.** The isosceles-triangle macro decomposed `משולש שווה שוקיים ABC` to `triangle ABC` + a **hard** `|AB|=|AC|` ("apex = first vertex"). But "isosceles" only asserts that **some** two sides are equal — *which* pair is the student's to state, not ours to assume. So `משולש שווה שוקיים ABC` followed by the student's own `AB=BC` stacked `|AB|=|AC|` **and** `|AB|=|BC|` → `|AB|=|AC|=|BC|` → an **equilateral** triangle the student never asked for (operator: "AB=BC doesn't mean the triangle is שווה צלעות"). The assumed pair is exactly the kind of unstated magnitude ADR-052 forbids pinning.

**Decision.** The macro's default equality is tagged `soft` on the `set-equal` command. In `replay` (the store's fact fold, where cross-fact resolution already lives — cf. the ADR-104 deferral), a pre-scan **drops a soft equality whose three vertices wholly contain another enabled, explicit (non-soft) `set-equal`** — so the student's stated pair becomes the isosceles pair and the assumed one steps aside (status `ok`, not pushed to the verifier's givens since it was never stated). Position-independent (works whether the explicit pair is typed before or after the shape word). With **no** explicit pair the soft default stands, so a lone `משולש שווה שוקיים ABC` still draws isosceles (`|AB|=|AC|`, verified). The engine ignores the `soft` flag (treats it as an ordinary equality) — conflict resolution is purely a fact-orchestration concern. Scoped to the isosceles triangle (the one genuinely ambiguous pair); `equilateral` (all three equal — unambiguous), `kite`, and `isosceles trapezoid` (pairs fixed by the labelling/parallel-side convention) stay hard, and the `soft` mechanism is reusable if another ambiguous-pair shape ever needs it.

**Result.** `משולש שווה שוקיים ABC` + `AB=BC` now builds isosceles with `|AB|=|BC|` and `|AC|` distinct (not equilateral); the lone isosceles still reads `|AB|=|AC|`. Locked by scenario `isosceles-explicit-pair-overrides-default` (the operator's exact sequence — incl. the two medians + their intersection). **1324 tests green, build clean.** *Follow-up (not built): when no pair is stated, "show another configuration" could cycle WHICH pair is the equal one (3 isosceles configs) — a fuller ADR-052 treatment of the ambiguity.*

## ADR-115 — A tangency declared against an EXISTING circle is a constraint, never a re-built circle

**Status.** Accepted (2026-06-24). Applies the [ADR-075](#adr-075)/[ADR-082](#adr-082) "existing object → constraint, not reconstruction" principle (and [ADR-099](#adr-099)'s inscribe-in-existing-circle) to the two rules that build a circle as a *side-effect of a tangency relation*. Operator-reported from session `fn2wt71w` (kite + inscribed triangle + two tangents); its sibling found by a debug-log audit of the whole `'X' is already defined` error class.

**Context.** Two parser rules construct a circle whose centre/radius are *derived from a tangency*: `cornerTangentCircle` ("AB ו-AD משיקים למעגל O" — two sides tangent to a circle) and `incircle` ("משולש DEF חוסם את המעגל O" — a triangle circumscribing a circle). Both auto-named the circle's centre from the utterance (`circleCenter`) **without checking whether that circle already exists**. When the student names a circle that is already in the figure (the two-tangents-from-A kite: triangle BCD inscribed in O, then AB/AD tangent to O), the rule rebuilt O — a free centre on the angle bisector + a `circle-through` the tangent foot — which **re-radiused the existing circle** and kicked its inscribed points off it. The original symptom was a hard `'O' is already defined` crash; that turned out to be a **stale dev server** (the running server predated the [ADR-107](#adr-107) placement-family fix that now absorbs the redefine). On current HEAD the crash was gone but the figure was **silently wrong — verifier amber** ("B should lie on circle O (radius 1.84) but is 4.25 from centre"). An audit of all 11 historical `already defined` sequences confirmed 9 were already fixed by later ADRs and these two shared one root pattern.

**Decision.** Each rule gains an **existing-circle guard**: when `circleCenter(s)` names a circle already in `ctx.circles`, emit a tangency **constraint** against it instead of constructing a fresh circle.
- **`cornerTangentCircle`** (two arms sharing a vertex): each arm is tangent at its **tip** (the non-shared endpoint = the touch point; the shared vertex is the external apex, off the circle). Put the tip on the circle if it isn't already a member, then `set-perpendicular(O, tip, vertex, tip)` — radius O–tip ⟂ the arm. Generalises to N arms. (Identical shape to `tangentLine`'s existing-line branch.)
- **`incircle`** (triangle circumscribing the circle): build the **dual** — three FREE touch points on the existing circle, the (scaffolding) tangent line at each, and the named vertices as the **pairwise tangent intersections** (`line-intersection`), then the triangle's drawn sides. This is **deterministic** (no coupled solve). The naïve alternative — forcing the foot of ⟂ from O onto each side onto the circle — *looks* right (and a quick check showed `violations: []`) but **over-constrains the per-constraint driver on the third side** (the check missed it by inspecting `violations` but not per-step `status` — a reminder that a green verifier is necessary but not sufficient).

**Result.** Both figures build correct and **verified** (the ADR-053 verifier re-derives tangency *and* the inscribed-points' membership, so green now means the circle keeps its members AND each side is tangent). `cornerTangentCircle` emits `segment, set-perpendicular ×2`; `incircle` emits the touch-points/tangents/intersections. Locked by scenarios `corner-tangent-on-existing-circle` (the operator's exact sequence; the kite step is the LLM decomposition the log shows) and `triangle-circumscribes-existing-circle`. **1326 tests green, build clean.** *Method note: the fix began by scanning `logs/debug-log.jsonl` for the whole error class and replaying each sequence against HEAD — the right first move before fixing, since most historical instances were already resolved and the live ones were two siblings, not one bug.*

## ADR-115 Am. — The existing-circle tangency guard resolves the circle implicitly (single circle)

**Status.** Accepted (2026-06-24, amends [ADR-115](#adr-115)). Operator-reported from session `6ai22ulh` (bagrut Q4).

**Context.** ADR-115's existing-circle guard on `cornerTangentCircle` fired only when the circle was **named** (`circleCenter(s)` ∈ `ctx.circles`). The student typed `AB ו AD משיקים למעגל` — "AB and AD tangent to **the** circle", no name — with one circle (O) already present. The guard missed it, so it fell through to the build-a-new-corner-circle path, which created spurious feet **E**, **K** and an auxiliary circle **P** — **hijacking the label E**, the very point the figure needed for "המשך BO חותך את המעגל בנקודה E". The arc/angle constraint then referenced a pinned tangent-foot E that "would not move".

**Decision.** Resolve the circle the same way ADR-029 resolves an implicit reference: the **named** circle if it exists, **else THE one circle** when the figure has exactly one. `cornerTangentCircle` now constrains the existing circle (no points created) for both `…למעגל O` and the unnamed `…למעגל` with a single circle present; it builds a fresh corner circle only when **no** circle is in context.

**Result.** "AB ו AD משיקים למעגל" emits `segment, set-perpendicular ×2` against circle O and creates **no** spurious points, so E stays free for its real definition. Locked by scenario `arc-ratio-and-implicit-tangent-q4` (asserts K, P do not exist).

## ADR-116 — Arc-measure relations (`קשת`/arc) map to the central-angle ratio

**Status.** Accepted (2026-06-24). Operator-reported from session `6ai22ulh`: the bagrut Q4 textbook given is `⌢DE = 2⌢CE` (arc DE = 2·arc CE) and there was **no arc term**, so it could not be entered.

**Context.** Israeli bagrut figures state circle givens as arc-measure relations (`⌢DE = 2⌢CE`, `קשת AB = קשת CD`). The vocabulary had `arc-midpoint` / "on arc" (ADR-042) but **no arc-measure relation**. A student had to hand-translate it to central angles `∠DOE = 2∠COE` — error-prone, and it referenced the wrong (hijacked) E in the corpus session.

**Decision.** A new `arcEquality` parser rule: an arc's measure equals its **central angle**, so arc XY on the circle centred at O ≡ ∠XOY, and `⌢DE = 2⌢CE` becomes the engine's existing `set-angle-ratio` on the two central angles (∠DOE = 2·∠COE) — the same relation `angleEquality` (ADR-100) emits, so on an under-determined figure it **drives** a free on-circle DOF until the arcs hold, and on a determined one the givens verifier **checks** it. Bilingual: `קשת` / `arc` / the `⌢` combining symbol, equality (k=1) or ratio (`= 2 …`). The circle is resolved implicitly (ADR-029: named, or the one circle); with no circle the rule defers (→ LLM). No radii are drawn — the arc lives on the circle boundary, not as central radii (matches the textbook figure); the arc endpoints are assumed on that circle (true in the corpus). Registered before `angleEquality` (its own `arc`/`קשת` keyword). Catalogued (`arc DE = 2 arc CE in circle O`).

**Result.** All forms parse to the central-angle ratio; the clean Q4 build satisfies arc DE = 2·arc CE (verified: ∠DOE = 2∠COE holds). Locked by scenario `arc-ratio-and-implicit-tangent-q4`. **1327 tests green, build clean.** *Follow-up (not built): visible arc marks for the two arcs, and minor/major-arc disambiguation when a reflex arc is intended (the central angle uses the ≤180° reading).*

## ADR-117 — A named triangle shape on an inscribe carries its equal-side relations

**Status.** Accepted (2026-06-24). Extends [ADR-110](#adr-110) (named-shape macros) to compose with `inscribedPolygon`. Operator-reported from session `dhhj7wo3` (bagrut figure).

**Context.** `ABC משולש שווה צלעות חסום במעגל` ("equilateral triangle ABC inscribed in a circle") built a **generic** inscribed triangle — three on-circle vertices + a triangle, with **no equal-side constraints** — so the triangle was not equilateral. The `inscribedPolygon` rule detects QUAD shapes (square/rectangle/rhombus/trapezoid, via `kind` → fixed `INSCRIBED_ANGLES`) but had no handling for the TRIANGLE shape words; and because "שווה צלעות"/"equilateral" is **not** a `SHAPE_LEFTOVER` token, the qualifier neither constrained the figure nor triggered the compound→LLM escalation — it was **silently dropped**. The standalone macros (`equilateral`, `isoscelesTriangle`) defer to `inscribedPolygon` for the inscribed case (`triThroughCircle` only defers the *circumscribes/through* phrasing; "חסום"/inscribed is meant to be owned downstream), so nothing added the equal sides.

**Decision.** `inscribedPolygon` detects an equilateral/isosceles qualifier on a triangle and appends the **same** equal-side relations the standalone macros emit — equilateral = `|AB|=|BC|` + `|BC|=|CA|`; isosceles = a **soft** `|AB|=|AC|` (ADR-114, yields to an explicit pair). The constraints are appended to whichever branch builds (fresh circle, existing named circle, or the all-vertices-exist circumcircle), and the shape words are added to the rule's strip so they don't leak into the leftover. The inscribed vertices are free on-circle DOFs, so the constraint solver flexes them into shape (an equilateral inscribed triangle: verified `|AB|=|BC|=|CA|`).

**Result.** "equilateral/isosceles triangle inscribed in a circle" (He + En) now builds the correct shape on the circle, verifier green; a plain "triangle inscribed in a circle" is unchanged (generic). Locked by scenario `equilateral-triangle-inscribed` (the operator's exact utterance) + parser-coverage entries. **1332 tests green, build clean.** *Note: equilateral could alternatively be deterministic fixed angles (120° apart, like `square`); the constraint form was chosen so it composes uniformly across all three branches (incl. existing/already-placed vertices) and reuses the macro relations.*

## ADR-118 — Area as a first-class measure & constraint (given / ratio / labelled)

**Status.** Accepted (2026-06-24). Operator-requested after a manual session raised "area of BDE = 13"; scoped by reading how area is *given* across two real bagrut documents (a 24-page worksheet + the 246-page 2025 booklet `571`).

**Context.** Israeli bagrut geometry/trig problems routinely state **area** givens, in several shapes (triangle, quad, kite, rectangle, parallelogram, trapezoid, rhombus) and several phrasings: an **absolute** value (`שטח המלבן הוא 4`, `שטח הדלתון הוא 25√3`, `area = p²`), a **ratio** (`SABC/SDEF = 3/4`, `גדול פי 2 מ` = 2× larger, `רבע מ` = a quarter of, `היחס בין שטח X ובין שטח Y הוא 1.8`), and an area **labelled with a letter** (`נסמן את שטח הדלתון ABCD ב-S`, then referenced later). Area had no representation, so all of these escalated or were dropped.

**Key principle (scale vs. shape).** Area has dimension *length²*. A **lone absolute area only pins the figure's SCALE** — and because the tool draws every figure up to similarity (the renderer fits to viewport, ADR-101), pinning scale produces *no visible shape change*: an equilateral triangle stays equilateral, just resized so its area is 13. Area drives a SHAPE DOF only when it becomes **dimensionless** — a ratio, or a second absolute given. This is exactly the existing treatment of an absolute length (the first `AB = 6` pins scale, the second shapes), so area joins the same machinery, not a parallel one.

**Decision.** Area is modelled as a measure, mirroring length/angle (ADR-031):
- **Engine.** `geometry.polygonArea` (shoelace); two constraints `area` (= value) and `area-ratio` (area(P₁) = k·area(P₂)) with `residual`/`constraintScale`/`tolerance`/`describe` in `solve.ts` (length²-scaled, relative to the target area); `set-area` / `set-area-ratio` commands routed through the existing `driveOrCheck` in `apply.ts`. No new solver — the area residual is driven like any other.
- **Symbolic layer.** A `measure-area` `SymbolicCommand` lowered (`lower.ts`) exactly like `measure-length`: a value → `set-area`; a **shared variable** → `set-area-ratio` against the representative (so `SABC = 2S`, `SDEF = S` couple two areas); the representative / a lone `SABC = S` stays a free label. RHS scope (operator-chosen): number, radical (`25√3`), variable, and power (`p²`); full trig expressions deferred.
- **Parser.** One `area` rule (registered BEFORE the shape rules, so a verbose `שטח המשולש ABC …` is read as an area, not built as a triangle): reads area references in compact `SABC` or verbose `שטח [ה<shape>] ABC` / `area [of] [the] [<shape>] ABC`; two refs → `set-area-ratio` with k from the connective (`/`+`= r`, `פי k`, `רבע/שליש/חצי`, `היחס…הוא r`, a bare coefficient); one ref + value/variable → `measure-area`. (Two Hebrew-specific traps fixed: JS `\b` doesn't fire around non-ASCII, so `הוא`/`רבע` are matched without it; and references read only the LEADING vertex run so a later filler word — the literal "area" — isn't misread as labels A,R,E,A.)
- **Verify / render / DOF.** `verify.ts` re-derives `area`/`area-ratio` (green = the stated area actually holds); the store builds an `areas` label map and the renderer prints it at the polygon's centroid; `similarityGauge` counts an absolute `area` as pinning scale (like `distance`), so the DOF cue does NOT double-count (a lone area on an equilateral-inscribed figure stays 0; on a free triangle stays at its shape DOF).

**Result.** All formats found in the two documents parse and build verified — absolute (free/inscribed), the two-areas-in-one-figure case, ratios (`/`, `פי k`, `רבע`, `היחס…`), and the letter label — bilingual + compact `S`-notation. A lone area sets size and keeps shape; a ratio reshapes. Locked by scenarios `area-absolute-sets-scale-not-shape` + `area-ratio-reshapes` and parser-coverage entries; catalogued. **1344 tests green, build clean.** *Deferred: cross-kind symbolic coupling (`area = p²` together with a length `OE = p` doesn't yet couple area to the length), and the full trig-expression RHS (`√2·k²·sinα/…`, answer-check territory).*

## ADR-119 — A chord's endpoints lie on the circle in ANY phrasing (membership survives a relation)

**Status.** Accepted (2026-06-25). Operator-reported from session `sflkyd0r` (manual testing, work PC).

**Context.** After `AB קוטר במעגל` (circle O + diameter AB), the input `CD ו AF מיתרים המקבילים זה לזה` ("CD and AF are chords parallel to each other") parsed to **two plain segments + `set-parallel`** — the `מיתר`/chord **membership was silently dropped**, so C, D, A, F were free points joined by parallel segments, *not* points on circle O. The standalone `chord` rule does place both endpoints on the circle, but two things defeat it once a relation is attached: (1) `parse` is **first-match-wins**, and `parallelConstraint` (which understands only plain segments) runs *before* the `chord` rule and claims the whole utterance; (2) even if reached, the `chord` rule handles only **one** chord (exactly two labels) and emits no relation. The same holds for `⟂` chords. This is a class bug ("a chord used inside any relation loses its circle"), not a one-off — so the fix must be general, not a per-relation special case.

**Decision.** A single centralised post-pass in `parse`, `withChordMembership(commands, s, ctx)`, applied to **every** matched parse (before `withImplicitCircles`): when the utterance is chord-flavoured (`chord`/`מיתר`) **and** a circle resolves (`resolveCenter` — a named circle or the figure's single circle), assert `point-on-circle` for **every `segment` endpoint** in the result. Keying off `segment` (the universal carrier the relational rules already emit for their operands) makes it uniform across parallel / ⟂ chords and any future relation that draws its operands. Safeguards: idempotent (the standalone `chord` rule's own membership is deduped); a circle **centre** is excluded (so "radius OE" keeps O off the circle, only E goes on); a chord's **midpoint** is never a *segment* endpoint, so `C אמצע מיתר AB` puts A,B — not C — on the circle. Matches the standalone rule's unconditional semantics (a chord's endpoints are on the circle whether they are new or already placed).

**Result.** `CD ו AF מיתרים המקבילים` now lands C, D, F on circle O (|OC|=|OD|=|OF|=r) with CD ∥ AF, verifier green; `⟂` chords and the singular `המיתר CD מקביל למיתר AF` phrasing behave the same; the single chord, midpoint-of-chord, radius-operand, and no-circle-context cases are unchanged (verified). Locked by scenario `parallel-chords-keep-circle-membership` (the operator's exact sequence). **1345 tests green, build clean.** *Known separate gap (surfaced, not fixed here): the WORD-form equality "X ו Y שווים" / "equal chords" isn't recognised as an equality at all — only the symbolic `AB = CD` is (`equalSegments`) — so "AB ו CD מיתרים שווים" still falls to the single-chord rule and drops the second chord + the equality. **Operator resolved this NON-actionable** (2026-06-25): students spell equality out as `AB=BC`, so word-form equality is not needed.*

## ADR-120 — The `△` triangle glyph as a parser keyword + toolbar button

**Status.** Accepted (2026-06-25). Operator-noted during manual testing: "do we support congruent/similar triangles? … we are missing the symbol of a triangle in the input symbols."

**Context.** Triangle **congruence** (`חופף` / congruent / `≅`) and **similarity** (`דומה` / similar / `~`) were already fully supported ([ADR-032](#adr-032)): the `congruence` rule reshapes the 2nd triangle to match the 1st by SSS, `similarity` by AA — verified end-to-end (`משולש ABC חופף למשולש GHT` builds both triangles and `|GH|=|AB|, |HT|=|BC|, |TG|=|CA|`). The toolbar already offered `≅` and `~`. Two gaps remained: (1) the textbook **triangle glyph `△`** was not in the symbol palette, so `△ABC ≅ △DEF` could only be typed by pasting; and (2) `△ABC` *alone* did not build a triangle (`not-handled`) — inconsistent with `∠ABC` (which states an angle), since `△` was not a recognised triangle keyword.

**Decision.** Treat `△` (and the filled `▲`) as the construction glyph for "triangle", the counterpart of `∠` for angles: (a) add a `△` button to the input symbol palette (`App.tsx` `SYMBOLS`, beside `≅`/`~`); (b) extend the base `triangle` rule keyword to `/triangle|משולש|[△▲]/` so `△ABC` builds a triangle; (c) add `△▲` to the `twoTriangles` strip so `△ABC ≅ △DEF` is read cleanly (it already worked because the label-triple regex skips the non-word glyph, but the strip is now explicit). Scoped to the *base* triangle (a bare `△ABC`); qualified shapes still need their word (`isosceles`, `right`, …). The relation rules (`congruence`/`similarity`) run before the shape rules, so `△ABC ≅ △DEF` is unaffected by (b).

**Result.** `△ABC` builds a triangle; `△ABC ≅ △DEF` / `△ABC ~ △DEF` work from the toolbar; the `חופף`/`דומה`/`congruent`/`similar` word forms are unchanged. Catalog congruence/similarity examples updated to the `△` form (so the coverage guard verifies `△` parses in both locales) and the triangle entry notes the `△ABC` shorthand. Locked by scenario `congruent-triangles-word-form` (the operator's exact `חופף` phrasing, build-level SSS) + catalog-coverage. **1346 tests green, build clean.**

## ADR-121 — Area subscript notation `S_{ABC}` / `S_ABC` (+ an `S_{}` toolbar button)

**Status.** Accepted (2026-06-25). Operator question during manual testing: "how would a student enter areas? should (can?) we have an `S_{}` format?". Operator chose scope = input notation + a toolbar button (not canvas subscript rendering).

**Context.** Area was already a first-class measure ([ADR-118](#adr-118)) with three entry forms: verbose (`שטח המשולש ABC הוא 13` / `area of triangle ABC = 13`), the **glued compact** `SABC = 13`, and a letter label (`נסמן את שטח ABCD ב-S`). But the textbook form a student actually reads is the **subscript** $S_{ABC}$ — and neither `S_{ABC} = 13` nor `S_ABC = 13` parsed (`not-handled`): the area rule's compact matcher (`/\bS((?:[A-Z]\d*){3,4})\b/`) and its gate (`/\bS[A-Z]/`) both require the vertices glued straight onto `S`, and the underscore breaks them. The glued form is the unintuitive part.

**Decision.** Accept the subscript forms by **normalising them to the glued form up front**, rather than touching the area rule: a `normalizeAreaSubscript` pass in `parse` (after `normalizeGreek`) rewrites `S_{ABC}` → `SABC` and `S_ABC` → `SABC`. Scoped to `S_` followed by **3–4 uppercase vertex labels** (`/\bS_\{((?:[A-Z]\d*){3,4})\}/` and the brace-less variant), so it can only ever touch area references (a 2-label `S_{AB}` or a non-area `S_1` is left alone), and every downstream area path (absolute, ratio, label) inherits it for free. Plus an `S_{}` button in the input symbol palette: `insertSymbol` gained an optional `caret` offset so the button inserts the `S_{}` template and drops the caret **between the braces** for the vertices. The glued `SABC` and verbose forms keep working unchanged.

**Result.** `S_{ABC} = 13`, `S_ABC = 13`, `S_{ABC}/S_{DEF} = 3/4`, `S_{ABCD} = 20`, and the `S_{ABC} = S` label all parse (the empty `S_{}` template no-ops as `not-handled` until vertices are typed); `AB = 6` and other non-area input are untouched. Catalog area examples switched to the `S_{ABC}` form (coverage guard verifies bilingual parse) and note all three input shapes + the button. Locked by `src/parser/__tests__/area-subscript.test.ts` (incl. the safe-scoping cases). **1355 tests green, build clean.** *Deferred (the scope option the operator did NOT pick): rendering the on-canvas area label as a true subscript $S_{ABC}$ — would reuse the `O1`→`O₁` presentation mechanism.*

**Amendment (2026-06-25, session `id4dn4a2`).** A ratio with a **coefficient glued to the second area marker** — `S_{ACD}=4S_{NCE}` (and the plain `SACD=4SNCE`) — failed to parse: both the area-ref regex and `normalizeAreaSubscript` required a **word boundary** before `S`, which the digit `4` breaks (digit↔`S` is not a `\b`). The spaced `S_{ACD}=4 S_{NCE}` always worked. Fixed by using `(?<![A-Za-z])S` (not `\bS`) in both — a coefficient (or any non-letter) before the marker is now fine; an `S` inside a word is still excluded. Locked by three new cases in `area-subscript.test.ts`. **1374 tests green, build clean.** *(This is the parse half of the operator's report; the apply half — the area ratio erroring "O and N would be at the same point" — is [ADR-123](#adr-123).)*

## ADR-122 — Swap two labels (A ↔ B), from the segment menu or typed; + a token-aware utterance relabel

**Status.** Accepted (2026-06-25). Operator-reported from a manual session (the chord figure): wanting C and D on opposite sides, "show another configuration" wouldn't do it, and renaming C→D errored; the workaround corrupted the labels.

**Context.** Two linked problems on the chord figure (diameter AB + parallel chords CD, AF). (1) **"Show another configuration" can't flip C and D** — and shouldn't: which endpoint is *labelled* C vs D is a **relabeling**, not a distinct geometric shape (the chord is the same undirected segment, the intersections sit on the same line), so the similarity-invariant resampler ([ADR-065](#adr-065)) correctly never offers it. The right tool is a label swap, which didn't exist. (2) **There was no swap, and rename can't do it**: `rename(C→D)` when D exists returns `target-taken` (a deliberate guard against an accidental merge — merge is separate), so the natural swap is blocked. The student's temp-label workaround (`D→C1`, `C→D`, …) then hit a real bug: the rename's *utterance* relabel did a **substring** replace — `f.utterance.split(F).join(T)` — so renaming `C`→`D` also rewrote the `C` inside `C1`, turning the displayed `CC1`→`DD1` (the *commands* stayed correct — they're renamed by point-id — so only the row text desynced, but visibly and confusingly).

**Decision.** (a) **A first-class `swap(a, b)` store action** — exchange two existing labels everywhere in one undoable step, built on the rename primitives via a `\0` sentinel so the two exchanges don't collide (`A→\0`, `B→A`, `\0→B`), across commands, utterances, hidden/segStyle/hiddenCircles. Because the on-circle sampler is order-keyed, relabeling actually exchanges the two points' positions, so C and D visibly switch sides (verified). (b) **A token-aware `relabelUtterance`** (`from + '(?!\\d)'`) replaces the substring `split/join` in both `rename` and `merge`, so a whole label is rewritten but never a letter inside a multi-char label (`C1`, `O1`). (c) **Surfaces:** the **segment menu** gets a "Swap endpoints" action (the natural place — you click the chord CD and swap its ends; `SceneSegment` now carries its endpoint ids `aId`/`bId`), AND a typed `parseSwap` — "swap C and D" / "swap C ↔ D" / Hebrew "החלף בין C ל-D" (the word בין marks a swap, so the plain "החלף E ב-G" replace-rename is untouched). The `swap` keyword was **removed from `parseRename`** (it used to live there, so "swap C and D" became `rename(C,D)` → the very `target-taken` error). The rename "taken" note now also points the student at swap.

**Result.** Clicking chord CD → "Swap endpoints" (or typing the command) exchanges C and D — they switch sides, figure stays valid, one undo entry; multi-char labels (`C1`) are never corrupted by a rename/swap. Locked by `src/store/__tests__/swap.test.ts` (the operator's figure: C↔D exchange positions, undo, plus the `C1` non-corruption regression) + `src/parser/__tests__/swap-parse.test.ts`. **1371 tests green, build clean.** *Note on Issue 1: "show another configuration" is intentionally left as-is — it explores shapes, not labelings; the swap is the correct tool for the C-vs-D question.*

## ADR-123 — A FORCED coincidence is allowed (with a notice), not a hard error

**Status.** Accepted (2026-06-25). Operator-directed, from the bagrut kite figure (session `id4dn4a2`). Supersedes the abandoned recruiter-tuning approach (see the investigation in [09-implementation-plan.md](09-implementation-plan.md)).

**Context.** On a kite ABCD inscribed in a circle, with E on DC, BE⟂DC, N = BE∩AC, the given `area(NCE) = ¼·area(ACD)` errored **"O and N would be at the same point"** on every seed. The investigation (worth recording, because the first hypotheses were wrong):
- △NCE ~ △ACD is **structural** — a cyclic kite is a *right kite* (∠ADC = 90°), and BE⟂DC gives ∠NEC = 90°, with a shared ∠C — so the area ratio ¼ is **exactly equivalent to the linear ratio CN/CA = ½** (the engine confirms the angle-triples match to <0.1° in every configuration).
- Because ∠ADC = 90°, by **Thales AC is a diameter**, so the circle's centre **O is the midpoint of AC** (verified `|O − mid(CA)| = 0`). Therefore `CN/CA = ½` places **N exactly on O**.
- The solver was **never the problem**: driving the figure to `CN/CA = 0.3 / 0.4 / 0.6 / 0.7` all converge cleanly; **only the ½ value fails**, and only because the coincidence guard hard-rejects the resulting N = O. The given simply *is* satisfied at a configuration where a derived point lands on the centre.

**Decision.** A coincidence that the **geometry/constraints genuinely drive** is a legitimate configuration, not a contradiction — so `evaluateCore` no longer hard-fails on it. It **collects** the non-intended coincident pairs and returns them as `coincidences` on the OK result; the store carries them on `Derived`; the app shows an **info notice** ("X and Y have landed at the same point"). Operator's rule, verbatim: *"you never put 2 nodes in the same position as a default unless the shape dictates it; if this happens, there will be a notice saying that both points converge — it's part of the building experimentation."* So **default** collisions are still avoided **upstream** (the sampler seeds points apart, ADR-060; shape composition flips to the non-stacking side and `chooseComposition` now prefers a *coincidence-free* placement, erroring only when both sides stack — a default collision it can't dodge). A `~`-scaffolding or `coincide`-constraint pair stays fully intended (no notice). Justification for this case: **O was never defined by the student** (an auto/hidden centre), so two labels meeting there is clearly fine.

**Result.** The operator's exact sequence now builds: `area(NCE) = ¼·area(ACD)` holds, **N converges onto O** (`|NO| = 0`), and a notice reports it — no error. Every non-degenerate ratio was already fine. The earlier abandoned approaches (sensitivity-filtered recruiter, similarity→side-ratio reduction) proved **unnecessary**: the recruiter/solver work; the only blocker was the over-strict guard. Locked by scenario `area-ratio-converges-points-allowed` (the operator's exact utterances). Composition's default-collision avoidance is preserved (`phase5-composition` green). **1375 tests green, build clean.** *Caveat: a coincidence that collapses a **constrained** segment still fails honestly at the constraint check (a zero-length distance / NaN direction is a genuine over-constraint), so this only relaxes the standalone "two points overlap" guard.*

**Amendment (2026-06-25, operator: "engine in a loop, page freezes").** Allowing the coincidence unmasked an **inconsistency that froze the app**: the figure-validity checks still treated the (now-allowed) coincidence as invalid. `meetsRequirements` (the auto-resolver's bar) required `pointsDistinct`, which N=O fails — so `autoResolve` → `findValidConfig` looped ~136 replays hunting a distinct-points configuration that **cannot exist** (the area ratio *forces* N=O), and each replay of this coupled figure is ~1.5–6 s → effectively a hang. Fix: `pointsDistinct` takes an `allowed` list (the figure's `coincidences`), so a FORCED coincidence is not an avoidable near-collision; `meetsRequirements` and `resample` pass `fig.coincidences`. Now `meetsRequirements` is true → `autoResolve` early-returns, no loop. Locked by `src/store/__tests__/coincidence.test.ts` (the `pointsDistinct` allow-list unit test + the operator's figure asserts `meetsRequirements === true`). **1378 tests green, build clean.** *Known perf follow-up (NOT the freeze): a single `replay` of this figure is ~1.5 s because the area-ratio recruits many DOFs (incl. the gauge centre/radius) into an expensive multi-start solve — bounded and usable now, but a leaner recruitment (or the similarity→side-ratio reduction) would speed it up; "show another configuration" iterates seeds so it is correspondingly slow on this figure.*

## ADR-124 — `extend-onto-circle` onto an EXISTING target point is a CONSTRAINT, never a silent no-op

**Status.** Accepted (2026-06-25, work PC). Operator-reported ("check why C-A-D was not respected in the last test"), diagnosed from the debug log (session `v7veg7sc`). The engine fix is already committed in `8b098fa` (a concurrent session committed the working-tree change verbatim — the commit message notes "ADR-124 doc entry to follow"); this is that entry. *Single file: `src/engine/apply.ts`.*

**Context.** The operator's bagrut figure (two circles O, P meeting at A, B): `המיתר AD במעגל P משיק למעגל O בנקודה A` ("chord AD tangent to O at A" — the LLM created **D** as a free on-circle point of P plus the tangency `OA ⟂ AD`), then later `המשך CA חותך את מעגל P בנקודה D` ("extend CA onto circle P at D"). The extension was **silently ignored**: the final figure satisfied the two tangencies but D sat wherever the tangent-chord step left it — replaying the operator's exact facts gives a C-A-D collinearity residual of **0.38** (nowhere near a straight line). Root cause: `extend-onto-circle` was written assuming its target point is **new**. Both code paths re-create the point with `addObj`, which **keeps the first definition** when the id already exists (the deterministic-id idempotency rule, `apply.ts:143`). The shared-endpoint path (case 1, taken here because A is an O∩P crossing → already on circle P) pushes **no constraints**, so when D pre-exists the whole directional coupling (collinear + beyond-the-2nd-letter order) is dropped to **nothing** — a complete no-op.

**Decision.** When the `extend-onto-circle` target **already exists**, treat the extension as a **constraint on that existing point**, never a re-build (a new case (0) before the existing case 1/2). Push `collinear(a, b, id)` + `collinear-order([a, b, id])` (id beyond the 2nd letter). If the existing point is a **free on-circle point of the same circle** (the chord-AD case), attach the order-aware far-crossing `solve` to its carrier — the very mechanism the fresh case-2 path uses — so its θ is driven onto line AB on the **far** side; for any other carrier the constraints alone flex the figure (the global solver / recruiter owns the DOF). Always draws the secant A→D. New targets keep the prior behaviour (case 1 deterministic shared-endpoint second-crossing, case 2 driven on-circle).

**Result.** Verified on a buildable variant (D pre-exists as a plain on-circle point, **no** conflicting tangency): the extension now lands D exactly on line CA, **beyond A** — collinear residual `0`, projection `1.955` → C-A-D order respected. Locked by a unit test in `src/engine/__tests__/extend-onto-circle.test.ts`. **1378 tests green, build clean.**

**The operator's *exact* figure is over-determined — now surfaced honestly, not hidden.** Proven two ways (the tangent-chord-angle algebra, and an independent 15,000-sample brute-force sweep importing **no** engine code): with **AD tangent to O at A** *and* **CB tangent to P at B**, the points **C, A, D can never be collinear** for any non-degenerate pair of circles (only the degenerate coincident-circles case, residual ~1e-10 at centre-gap ≈ 0). The algebra: tangent-chord at A ⟹ ∠DAB = ∠ACB; triangle ABC ⟹ ∠BAC = 180° − ∠ACB − ∠CBA; C-A-D collinear needs ∠BAC + ∠BAD = 180°, i.e. ∠BAD = ∠ACB + ∠CBA — but tangent-chord already fixed ∠BAD = ∠ACB, forcing ∠CBA = 0 (degenerate). So the explicit "AD tangent at A" pins D to a spot incompatible with the CA-extension. **Before this fix the tool hid the conflict** (a clean-looking figure that quietly violated C-A-D); **after it, the step reports `over-constrained: OA ⟂ AD cannot hold`** and keeps the prior figure — the correct, honest behaviour. Most likely the real problem gives "AD tangent to O at A" as the **conclusion to prove**, not a construction given; dropping it lets "extend CA onto P at D" build with C-A-D respected.
