**Parked (pending operator review of the bagrut source).** Whether the operator's *exact* (contradictory) sequence should (a) keep the honest over-constrained error or (b) be modelled with the tangency dropped is a problem-statement question the operator is checking ("let me look first"). The end-to-end regression scenario in `scenarios.test.ts` is **held** until that is decided; the engine behaviour itself is already locked by the `extend-onto-circle.test.ts` unit test above. *General principle reaffirmed: a derived/extension point that already exists is **re-constrained, not re-created** — the same lesson as `point-on-line`'s ADR-036 loose-point upgrade.*

## ADR-125 — A subject-named incentre ("M is the centre of the incircle") is honoured, not dropped

**Status.** Accepted (2026-06-25). Operator-reported, diagnosed from the debug log (session `djvbb7`): `M מרכז המעגל החסום במשולש BDC` ("M is the centre of the circle inscribed in triangle BDC") drew the centre at **O**, not M, and the tangency point on side BD showed as **G** and flipped to **F** when hidden. *Single file: `src/parser/parse.ts`.*

**Context.** The `incircle` rule named the incentre with `circleCenter(s)`, which only catches a centre phrased **"circle M" / "centred at M" / "מעגל M" / "שמרכזו M"** — a label that *follows* the centre word. The operator's phrasing puts the label as the **subject** ("**M** [is the] centre of …"), *before* the centre word, so `circleCenter` returned null → the incentre fell to the auto label `I` and **M was left unreferenced**. App.submit's dropped-label gate ([ADR-089](#adr-089)) then flagged the parse `weak:dropped:M` and escalated to the LLM, which built a **second, complete incircle** (centre O, foot G, `circle-O`) stacked on the parser's (centre I, foot F, `circle-I`). Hence all three symptoms from **one** cause — the wrong centre (the LLM's O won the display), and two duplicated tangency feet (G over F) that flip when one is hidden.

**Decision.** Add `incenterLabel(s)` — captures an incentre named as the **subject** of an incircle phrasing ("M מרכז…", "M is the [in]centre…", trailing "incentre M"). The `incircle` rule prefers `circleCenter` then `incenterLabel` (each guarded so it can't grab one of the triangle's own vertices), names the incentre and `circle-<that letter>`, and only sets `autoCenter` when the incentre is **un-named**. With the label honoured, nothing is dropped → no LLM escalation → exactly **one** incircle. (A symptom-level alternative — suppress the duplicate after the fact — was rejected per the no-patch rule; the root defect is the parser not reading the natural phrasing.)

**Also (same report, the hidden-circle half).** A hidden circle was drawn as a faint **dashed ghost** so it stayed clickable — the operator read it as "still there, just dashed". Since the circle group already carries an always-present transparent **hit-ring**, a hidden circle now draws **nothing** (truly invisible) while staying clickable on its former outline to un-hide (`src/render/Figure.tsx`). The incircle's tangency foot stays a normal, hideable point (operator: "ok to show it, but I need to hide it" — which works now that there's no duplicate underneath).

**Result.** `M מרכז המעגל החסום במשולש BDC` → incentre **M**, `circle-M`, a single foot, no escalation; M equidistant from the three sides. Locked by scenario `named-incenter-of-incircle` (the operator's exact `מלבן DCBA` / `BD` / `M מרכז…` sequence). **1380 tests green, build clean.**

## ADR-126 — "Two tangents from a point" works without naming the touch points

**Status.** Accepted (2026-06-25). Operator-reported, diagnosed from the debug log (session `gd0kkj`): `מנקודה A יוצאים שני משיקים למעגל O` ("from A, two tangents go out to circle O") fell to the LLM and built nothing — yet "a previous question worked nice". *Single file: `src/parser/parse.ts`.*

**Context.** The `tangentsFromExternal` rule (FR-EN-18) built the two tangents from an external point only when the **touch points were named** — `const abM = s.match(…"A and B"…); if (!abM) return null;`. The operator's previous, working question named them ("…בנקודות B ו C"); the natural phrasing that just says "two tangents from A to circle O" does **not**, so the rule bailed and the input escalated to the LLM (which produced nothing). Naming the touch points is incidental — the student wants the two tangent *lines* drawn, not to label where they touch.

**Decision.** When the touch points are not named, **auto-name two fresh ones** (dodging the figure's labels) and build the same Thales construction (the touch points lie on circle O ∩ the circle on diameter O-E, so each radius ⟂ its tangent). A named pair is still honoured (distinct from the apex, not the centre); only a genuinely absent/invalid pair triggers auto-naming. The rule was already correctly gated (tangent word + plural + "from <point>" + a resolvable circle), so reaching this point means it really is "two tangents from a point" — building is right, failing was the bug.

**Result.** `circle O radius 5` → `מנקודה A יוצאים שני משיקים למעגל O` now builds deterministically: two touch points on circle O and the two tangent segments from A, no LLM. The named form (and all existing tangent tests) are unchanged. Locked by scenario `two-tangents-from-point-unnamed-touch`. **1381 tests green, build clean.**

## ADR-127 — "The circumcircle cuts CE at D" keeps D ON the segment, in every configuration

**Status.** Accepted (2026-06-25). Operator-reported during manual testing ("all worked well but the last view violates the rule that D is on CE"). *Files: `src/parser/parse.ts`, `src/engine/apply.ts`, `src/engine/types.ts`.*

**Context.** `circumcircleMeetsSegment` ("המעגל החוסם את משולש ABC חותך את CE בנקודה D") built D as a `line-circle-intersection` of the **infinite** line through C,E with the circumcircle, **avoiding the shared vertex** — but with **no order constraint**. So the default seed happened to land D on the chord, while "show another configuration" (a re-sampled config) slid D onto the *extension* of CE (projection parameter t up to ~4.3). This violates the segment-reference principle (CLAUDE.md / ADR-077: "a segment reference means the segment between its endpoints, not the continuation, unless the student says המשך").

**Two-step diagnosis.** The first fix — appending `set-line [C,D,E]` — **failed at apply**: `set-line` runs `addCollinear`, which searches its three points for a free on-circle carrier to drive and mis-picks the chord endpoint **C** (a free on-circle point), then tries the second-intersection conversion on it → "unresolved dependencies for E, D, line-CE, line-CD" (a dependency cycle). The real insight: D is **already collinear** with C,E (it's a point *on* line CE), so it needs **only the side/order**, never `addCollinear`.

**Decision.** Give `line-circle-intersection` an optional `order` field (e.g. `[C, D, E]`). When present, apply pushes a single `collinear-order` constraint and nothing else (no `addCollinear`, no carrier re-marking). The order residual is folded into the joint minimisation (like every order/inequality constraint, ADR-039), so the solver/sampler flexes the figure's free DOFs (here the chord endpoints on circle O) to keep D between C and E **in every configuration**, not just the default seed. `circumcircleMeetsSegment` now emits `order: [shared, D, other]` on the intersection instead of a separate `set-line`.

**Result.** D lands on segment CE (t ∈ [0.12, 0.88]) across all sampled seeds (was -2.1 … +4.3); collinear residual 0, no error, no violation. Locked by scenario `circumcircle-cuts-segment-d-on-side`. **1381 tests green, build clean.** *Note: the operator's actual run had a typo ("חותרך") that escalated to the LLM (which produced the un-ordered decomposition); the deterministic rule — where this fix lives — fires on the correct spelling. A future catalog/LLM-prompt note could teach the `order` field to the fallback.*

## ADR-128 — Production LLM proxy: a shared handler hosted by one standalone Node server, deployed at the `/geo-builder/` subpath

**Status.** Accepted (2026-06-26). Phase A of the long-parked "production proxy deploy" thread (the only remaining gate before go-live; the static SPA was always trivial to serve). Operator decisions: live at **`themathbible.com/geo-builder/`** (a subpath under `httpdocs`, not a subdomain) and run the proxy as a **Node systemd service on the existing server** (mirroring the Flask apps). *Files: `server/parseHandler.ts` (new), `server/standalone.ts` (new), `server/build.mjs` (new), `server/llmProxy.ts` (refactor), `server/__tests__/parseHandler.test.ts` (new), `vite.config.ts`, `src/parser/llm.ts`, `package.json`, `.gitignore`.*

**Context.** The Phase-7 LLM fallback (ADR-023) existed **only as a Vite dev-server plugin** (`server/llmProxy.ts`, `POST /api/parse`) — there was no production entry point for the `/api/parse` proxy, and the comment "extractable to a serverless function" was never realised. The proxy holds `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, which must never reach the browser. Both a Vite middleware and a bare `http.createServer` callback receive Node's `IncomingMessage`/`ServerResponse` — that **shared shape** means one handler can serve both hosts, so dev and prod stay byte-identical (no drift between "what we tested locally" and "what runs live").

**Decision.**
1. **One shared handler** — `server/parseHandler.ts` `handleParse(req, res, { apiKey, logError })` holds all the logic (method guard, missing-key 503, per-IP rate limit, 4 KB body cap, dynamic Anthropic import, Haiku call via the pure `llmShared`). The dev plugin and the production server both call it; neither owns request logic.
2. **Standalone production server** — `server/standalone.ts`, a bare-`http` listener on `127.0.0.1:8788` (loopback only; reachable solely through the reverse proxy). It reads the key from `process.env` (a root-only systemd `EnvironmentFile`), serves `GET /healthz`, and matches **any path ending in `/api/parse`** so it works whether nginx strips the `/geo-builder` prefix or forwards it intact.
3. **Single-file deploy artifact** — `server/build.mjs` (esbuild, new `build:proxy` script) bundles the server **and the Anthropic SDK** into one self-contained `dist-server/proxy.mjs` (~504 KB). The server needs only Node + the env var — **no `npm install` on the box**. `dist-server` is gitignored.
4. **Subpath wiring** — `vite.config.ts` sets `base: '/geo-builder/'` for `command === 'build'` only (dev stays at root), and the client's fetch becomes base-relative (`` `${import.meta.env.BASE_URL}api/parse` ``) so it resolves to `/api/parse` in dev and `/geo-builder/api/parse` in prod with no per-environment branching. `sessionLog`'s `/api/log` is fully `import.meta.env.DEV`-gated, so it no-ops in production untouched.

**Why a standalone bundle over serverless / over keeping node_modules.** The operator wants everything on `themathbible.com` (no second provider), and the box already runs persistent services via systemd — so the Flask pattern applies, just `node` instead of `gunicorn`. Bundling the SDK in makes the deploy a two-file `scp` (`dist/` + `proxy.mjs`) with zero server-side dependency management, and keeps the key in a root-only env file (never in the bundle, never in git — the standing rule).

**Result.** `npm run build` clean (subpath confirmed in `dist/index.html`); `npm run build:proxy` produces the bundle; smoke test of the running bundle **with no key**: `/healthz` → 200, `POST /api/parse` → 503 `no-api-key` (proving it never fires a live call), the `/geo-builder/api/parse` prefix form → matched, `GET` → 405. Handler guards locked by `server/__tests__/parseHandler.test.ts` (method / 503 / 413 / empty / 429 rate-limit — all short-circuiting before the SDK, no autonomous API calls). **1387 tests green, build clean.**

**Deployed 2026-06-26 (Phases B + C, live at `https://themathbible.com/geo-builder/`).** Server reality differed from the plan in one way that matters for any future deploy to this box: **nginx is disabled/off** (failed since 2026-04) — themathbible.com serves over **HTTPS via Apache directly**, and all the existing app reverse-proxies (`/hw/`→8000, `/akinator`→5000, `/bagrut`→3000, `/akinator2`→5001) live in the **HTTPS Apache include** `vhost_ssl.conf`. So the `vhost_nginx.conf` route was a no-op (reverted to byte-identical original); the proxy is instead two `ProxyPass`/`ProxyPassReverse` lines appended to `vhost_ssl.conf` (backup → `apache2ctl -t` → `systemctl reload apache2`). The proxy runs as `geo-proxy.service` (User=www-data, loopback `127.0.0.1:8788`) from `/var/www/geo-proxy/` (outside the Plesk vhost, like the box's `math-evaluator`); the key is `/var/www/geo-proxy/geo-proxy.env` (root:root 600 — systemd reads it as root, www-data never sees it). Static SPA at `httpdocs/geo-builder/` (root:root 755/644, like the other static subdirs — served by Apache default, no directive). `mod_proxy_http` sets `X-Forwarded-For` automatically, so the per-client rate-limit works. Public verification (no live API call): static root 200, assets 200, `POST …/api/parse` empty body → 400 `empty` (Apache→node chain proven, key loaded), existing sites unaffected (`/akinator` 200, `/bagrut` 308). Deploy artifacts updated to match: `deploy/apache-geo-builder.conf` (replaces the nginx snippet), `deploy/README.md`. **Remaining: the operator-authorised live-Haiku browser test** (an out-of-grammar utterance) and the Console spend cap. **Persistence caveat:** the `vhost_ssl.conf` lines are direct edits — a Plesk domain reconfigure could drop them (static app keeps working; LLM fallback degrades gracefully); re-append or add via the Plesk GUI.
